\displaystyle=\begin{cases}3\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{2}+\tilde{O}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3}),&\text{if}\ i=j=k=l,\\
\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{2}+\tilde{O}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3}),&\text{if}\ i=k\neq j=l,\\
\tilde{O}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3}),&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Note that all derivatives above are w.r.t.
\tilde{x}_{t}
. All the rates are under
5
.

Proof.

See
Section

F.7
.
∎

Lemma

8
also justifies the expression of
\mu_{t}
and
\Sigma_{t}
in the diffusion process (i.e., (
3
) and (
4
)), which match the posterior mean and variance, respectively.

Next we turn to calculate the fifth and sixth centralized moment under
Q_{t-1|t}
, again drawing the idea of Tweedie’s formula
[
31
]
. This is a direct extension to
Lemma

8
.

Lemma 9
.

Fix
t\geq 1
such that
q_{t-1}
exists. Fix
x_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Under
5
, with the same definitions of
\tilde{x}_{t}
and
\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})
as in
Lemma

8
, the fifth centralized moment is

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}^{i}-\mu_{t}^{i})(X_{t-1}^{j}-\mu_{t}^{j})(X_{t-1}^{k}-\mu_{t}^{k})(X_{t-1}^{l}-\mu_{t}^{l})(X_{t-1}^{m}-\mu_{t}^{m})\right]\\
=\sum_{\xi\in\binom{\{i,j,k,l,m\}}{2}}(\partial^{2}_{\xi}\kappa)(\partial^{3}_{\{i,j,k,l,m\}\setminus\xi}\kappa)+\partial^{5}_{ijklm}\kappa=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4})

where, given a set
A
, we define

\binom{A}{2}:=\Big\{\{a_{1},a_{2}\}:a_{1},a_{2}\in A,\penalty\ a_{1}\neq a_{2}\Big\}.

Let
P_{n}^{k}
be the set that contains all distinct size-
k
partitions of
[n]
. Define

\mathrm{part}_{2}(A):=\{((a_{i},a_{j}):\{i,j\}\in p):p\in P_{\left|A\right|}^{2}\}.

The sixth centralized moment is

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}^{i}-\mu_{t}^{i})(X_{t-1}^{j}-\mu_{t}^{j})(X_{t-1}^{k}-\mu_{t}^{k})(X_{t-1}^{l}-\mu_{t}^{l})(X_{t-1}^{m}-\mu_{t}^{m})(X_{t-1}^{n}-\mu_{t}^{n})\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\xi_{3})\in\mathrm{part}_{2}(\{i,j,k,l,m,n\})}(\partial^{2}_{\xi_{1}}\kappa)(\partial^{2}_{\xi_{2}}\kappa)(\partial^{2}_{\xi_{3}}\kappa)+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4})

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}15\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{3}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}),&\text{if}\ i=j=k=l=m=n\\
3\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{3}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}),&\text{if}\ i=k=m=n\neq j=l\\
\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{3}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}),&\text{if}\ i=l,j=m,k=n\penalty\ \text{while $i,j,k$ all differ}\\
\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}),&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

Again note that all derivatives above are w.r.t.
\tilde{x}_{t}
.

Proof.

See
Section

F.8
.
∎

The following lemma provides the correct order (in terms of
(1-\alpha_{t})
) for all higher-order posterior centralized moments. In other words, this shows that
Q_{t-1|t}
has nice Gaussian-like concentration.

Lemma 10
.

Fix
t\geq 1
and
p\geq 2
. Let
\bm{a}=(a_{1},\dots,a_{p})\in[d]^{p}
be a vector of indices of length
p
. Under the same conditions as in Lemma
8
, if
p
is odd,

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1,t}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{p}(X_{t-1}^{a_{i}}-\mu_{t}^{a_{i}})\right]=\tilde{O}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{\frac{p+3}{2}}\right),\penalty\ \forall\bm{a}\in[d]^{p}.

(24)

If
p
is even,

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1,t}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{p}(X_{t-1}^{a_{i}}-\mu_{t}^{a_{i}})\right]=\tilde{O}((1-\alpha_{t})^{\frac{p}{2}}),\penalty\ \forall\bm{a}\in[d]^{p}.

(25)

Proof.

See
Section

F.9
.
∎

D.6
Step 5: Bounding term 3 – Reverse-step Error

We are now ready to assemble the respective moments into the final convergence rate. In the following lemma, we use the results in the previous lemmas to control the difference
\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}[\zeta^{\prime}_{t,t-1}]-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}[\zeta^{\prime}_{t,t-1}]
in (
D.3
).

Lemma 11
.

Suppose that
5
holds and that
q_{t-1}
exists. Then,

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}\right)[\zeta^{\prime}_{t,t-1}]\\
=\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{3}}{3!\alpha_{t}^{3/2}}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}[\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(X_{t})]+\tilde{O}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}).

Proof.

See
Section

F.10
.
∎

Therefore, under
2
and
5
we combine
Lemma

11
and (
D.3
) and get

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[\log\frac{q_{t-1|t}(X_{t-1}|X_{t})}{p^{\prime}_{t-1|t}(X_{t-1}|X_{t})}\right]\\
\lesssim(1-\alpha_{t})^{3}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}[\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(X_{t})].

(26)

This completes the proof of
Theorem

1
.

Before we end this section, we provide an upper bound of the reverse-step error when the conditional covariance of
P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}
is slightly perturbed (see
Remark

3
).

Corollary 3
.

Suppose that
5
holds and that
q_{t-1}
exists. Suppose that the conditional covariance of
P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}
is slightly perturbed, which satisfies

\tilde{\Sigma}_{t}(x_{t})=\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\left(I_{d}+A_{t}(x_{t})+\Xi_{t}(x_{t})\right),

where
\Xi_{t}(X_{t})=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{r}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\right)
for all
r\geq 1
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[\log\frac{q_{t-1|t}(X_{t-1}|X_{t})}{p^{\prime}_{t-1|t}(X_{t-1}|X_{t})}\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim-(1-\alpha_{t})\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))-\alpha_{t}\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right)\Xi_{t}(X_{t})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+(1-\alpha_{t})^{3}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}[\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(X_{t})]

\displaystyle=\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{2}}\right).

Proof.

See
Section

F.11
.
∎

Appendix E
Proof of
Corollary

1

Note that
q_{1}
always exists and is analytic by
Lemma

6
. Therefore, it remains to upper-bound the mismatch between
Q_{0}
and
Q_{1}
.
In the following lemma we provide such a common bound in Wasserstein distance, which is provided only for completeness.

Lemma 12
.

For any
Q_{0}
,

\mathrm{W}_{2}(Q_{0},Q_{1})^{2}\leq(1-\alpha_{1})(M_{2}+1)d.

Remark 4
.

If
1-\alpha_{1}=\delta
, this implies that

\mathrm{W}_{2}(Q_{0},Q_{1})^{2}\lesssim\delta d.

Proof.

See
Section

F.12
.
∎

The proof of this corollary is thus complete.
A consequence of Lemma
12
is that, in order to obtain convergence guarantees for general distributions, one can view
1-\alpha_{1}
as controlling the mismatch between
Q_{0}
and
Q_{1}
(in terms of the Wasserstein distance), and
1-\alpha_{t},\penalty\ \forall t\geq 2
as controlling the mismatch between
Q_{1}
and
\widehat{P}^{\prime}_{1}
(in terms of the KL-divergence).

Appendix F
Auxiliary Proofs for
Theorems

1
and
1

In this section, we provide the proofs for those auxiliary lemmas in the proof of
Theorems

1
and
1
.

F.1
Proof of
Lemma

3

First, note that

q_{T}(x_{T})=\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0}}[q_{T|0}(x_{T}|X_{0})].

Also note that the function
f(x)=x\log(x)
is convex. Thus, by Jensen’s inequality,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{T}\sim Q_{T}}\left[\log q_{T}(X_{T})\right]

\displaystyle=\int\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0}}[q_{T|0}(x_{T}|X_{0})]\log\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0}}[q_{T|0}(x_{T}|X_{0})]\mathrm{d}x_{T}

\displaystyle\leq\int\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0}}\left[q_{T|0}(x_{T}|X_{0})\log q_{T|0}(x_{T}|X_{0})\right]\mathrm{d}x_{T}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0}}\left[\int q_{T|0}(x_{T}|X_{0})\log q_{T|0}(x_{T}|X_{0})\mathrm{d}x_{T}\right].

Since
Q_{T|0}
is conditional Gaussian
\mathcal{N}(\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{T}}x_{0},(1-\bar{\alpha}_{T})I_{d})
, its negative conditional entropy equals

\int q_{T|0}(x_{T}|x_{0})\log q_{T|0}(x_{T}|x_{0})\mathrm{d}x_{T}=-\frac{d}{2}-\frac{d}{2}\log(2\pi(1-\bar{\alpha}_{T}))

for any
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. On the other hand, since
P^{\prime}_{T}=\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
,

\mathbb{E}_{X_{T}\sim Q_{T}}\left[\log p^{\prime}_{T}(X_{T})\right]=-\frac{d}{2}\log(2\pi)-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{X_{T}\sim Q_{T}}\left\lVert X_{T}\right\rVert^{2}

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{T}\sim Q_{T}}\left\lVert X_{T}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\bar{\alpha}_{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0}}\left\lVert X_{0}\right\rVert^{2}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{T})\mathbb{E}_{\bar{W}_{T}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\left\lVert\bar{W}_{T}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\bar{\alpha}_{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0}}\left\lVert X_{0}\right\rVert^{2}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{T})d.

Putting the two together,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{T}\sim Q_{T}}\left[\log\frac{q_{T}(X_{T})}{p^{\prime}_{T}(X_{T})}\right]=\mathbb{E}_{X_{T}\sim Q_{T}}\left[\log q_{T}(X_{T})\right]-\mathbb{E}_{X_{T}\sim Q_{T}}\left[\log p^{\prime}_{T}(X_{T})\right]

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{d}{2}-\frac{d}{2}\log(2\pi(1-\bar{\alpha}_{T}))+\frac{d}{2}\log(2\pi)+\frac{1}{2}\left(\bar{\alpha}_{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0}}\left\lVert X_{0}\right\rVert^{2}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{T})d\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\bar{\alpha}_{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0}}\left\lVert X_{0}\right\rVert^{2}-\frac{d\bar{\alpha}_{T}}{2}-\frac{d}{2}\log(1-\bar{\alpha}_{T}).

When
T
is large (and thus when
\bar{\alpha}_{T}
is small), the Taylor expansion w.r.t.
\bar{\alpha}_{T}
around 0 yields

\log(1-\bar{\alpha}_{T})=-\bar{\alpha}_{T}+O\left(\bar{\alpha}_{T}^{2}\right).

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{T}\sim Q_{T}}\left[\log\frac{q_{T}(X_{T})}{p^{\prime}_{T}(X_{T})}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\bar{\alpha}_{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0}}\left\lVert X_{0}\right\rVert^{2}-\frac{d\bar{\alpha}_{T}}{2}-\frac{d}{2}(-\bar{\alpha}_{T})+O\left(\bar{\alpha}_{T}^{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\bar{\alpha}_{T}M_{2}d+O\left(\bar{\alpha}_{T}^{2}\right).

F.2
Proof of
Lemma

4

To start, note that both
P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}
and
\widehat{P}^{\prime}_{t-1|t}
are Gaussian (yet having different mean
and variance
). Thus, for each
t=1,\dots,T
,

\displaystyle\log\frac{p^{\prime}_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})}{\widehat{p}^{\prime}_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})}

\displaystyle=\log\left(\det(\Sigma_{t})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)-\log\left(\det(\widehat{\Sigma}_{t})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\frac{1}{2}(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})^{\intercal}\Sigma_{t}^{-1}(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})+\frac{1}{2}(x_{t-1}-\widehat{\mu}_{t})^{\intercal}\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}(x_{t-1}-\widehat{\mu}_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\log(\det(\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}))-\log(\det(\Sigma_{t}))\right)+\frac{1}{2}(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})^{\intercal}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}-\Sigma_{t}^{-1})(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}(x_{t-1}-\widehat{\mu}_{t})^{\intercal}\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}(x_{t-1}-\widehat{\mu}_{t})-\frac{1}{2}(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})^{\intercal}\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left(\log(\det(\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}))-\log(\det(\Sigma_{t}))\right)+\frac{1}{2}(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})^{\intercal}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}-\Sigma_{t}^{-1})(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}(\mu_{t}-\widehat{\mu}_{t})^{\intercal}\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})+\frac{1}{2}(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})^{\intercal}\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}(\mu_{t}-\widehat{\mu}_{t})+\frac{1}{2}(\mu_{t}-\widehat{\mu}_{t})^{\intercal}\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}(\mu_{t}-\widehat{\mu}_{t}).

(27)

There are five terms in (
F.2
). We first consider the third and the fourth term, for which we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[(\mu_{t}-\widehat{\mu}_{t})^{\intercal}\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t})\right]=(\mu_{t}-\widehat{\mu}_{t})^{\intercal}\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[X_{t-1}-\mu_{t}\right]=0,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t})^{\intercal}\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}(\mu_{t}-\widehat{\mu}_{t})\right]=\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[X_{t-1}-\mu_{t}\right]^{\intercal}\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}(\mu_{t}-\widehat{\mu}_{t})=0.

Now consider the expectation of the last term in (
F.2
).
From the definition of
\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}
in (
6
), for small
1-\alpha_{t}
we have
\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}\succ 0
, and we can define
\widehat{B}_{t}:=I_{d}-(I_{d}+(1-\alpha_{t})H_{t})^{-1}
, and thus
\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}=\frac{\alpha_{t}}{1-\alpha_{t}}(I_{d}-\widehat{B}_{t})
. From Taylor expansion, we have
\widehat{B}_{t}=(1-\alpha_{t})H_{t}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{2})
.
Thus, for each
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[(\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t}))^{\intercal}\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}(X_{t})(\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t}))\right]

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha_{t})\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[(s_{t}(X_{t})-\nabla\log q_{t}(X_{t}))^{\intercal}(I_{d}-\widehat{B}_{t}(X_{t}))(s_{t}(X_{t})-\nabla\log q_{t}(X_{t}))\right]

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha_{t})\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[(s_{t}(X_{t})-\nabla\log q_{t}(X_{t}))^{\intercal}(I_{d}+(1-\alpha_{t})H_{t}(X_{t}))^{-1}(s_{t}(X_{t})-\nabla\log q_{t}(X_{t}))\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim(1-\alpha_{t})\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left\lVert s_{t}(X_{t})-\nabla\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}

where the last line follows from the regularity condition on
H_{t}
in
3
.
Therefore, the expectation of the last term in (
F.2
) can be bounded as

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[(\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t}))^{\intercal}\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}(X_{t})(\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\widehat{\mu}_{t}(X_{t}))\right]

\displaystyle\lesssim\sum_{t=1}^{T}(1-\alpha_{t})\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left\lVert s_{t}(X_{t})-\nabla\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(\log T)\varepsilon^{2},

(28)

where the last line follows by the score estimation error in
3
.

Next we turn to the first two terms in (
F.2
). First, note that for all
i,j\in[d]
, we have
(1-\alpha_{t})H_{t}^{ij}(X_{t})=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}(1-\alpha_{t})
under

3
.
Now, the first term of (
F.2
) is given by

\log(\det(\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}))-\log(\det(\Sigma_{t}))=\log(\det(I_{d}+(1-\alpha_{t})H_{t}))-\log(\det(I_{d}+(1-\alpha_{t})\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t}))).

When
(1-\alpha_{t})
is small, we can use Taylor expansion for the functions
\det(\cdot)
and
\log(\cdot)
to get

\displaystyle\log(\det(I_{d}+(1-\alpha_{t})H_{t}))

\displaystyle=\log\left(1+(1-\alpha_{t})\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t})+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2}(\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t})^{2}-\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t}^{2}))+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3})\right)

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha_{t})\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t})+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2}(\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t})^{2}-\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t}^{2}))-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2}\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t})^{2}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3})

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha_{t})\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t})-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2}\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t}^{2})+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3}).

Similar expression can be obtained for
\log(\det(I_{d}+(1-\alpha_{t})\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t})))
. Thus, the first term in (
F.2
) is equal to

\displaystyle\log(\det(\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}))-\log(\det(\Sigma_{t}))

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha_{t})\left(\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t})-\mathrm{Tr}(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t}))\right)-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2}\left[\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t}^{2})-\mathrm{Tr}((\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t}))^{2})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3}).

For the second term in (
F.2
), we first take expectation over
x_{t-1}
and get

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t})^{\intercal}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}-\Sigma_{t}^{-1})(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t})\right]=\mathrm{Tr}\left((\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}-\Sigma_{t}^{-1})\Sigma_{t}\right).

To proceed, note that

(I_{d}+(1-\alpha_{t})H_{t})^{-1}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{=}}I_{d}-(1-\alpha_{t})H_{t}+(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}H_{t}^{2}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3}).

(29)

To see
(iii)
, we write
S_{t}
as the true inverse of
I_{d}+(1-\alpha_{t})H_{t}
. Its existence is guaranteed if
(1-\alpha_{t})
is small. Since

(I_{d}+(1-\alpha_{t})H_{t})(I_{d}-(1-\alpha_{t})H_{t}+(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}H_{t}^{2})=I_{d}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3}),

we have

(I_{d}+(1-\alpha_{t})H_{t})(I_{d}-(1-\alpha_{t})H_{t}+(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}H_{t}^{2}-S_{t})=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3})

which implies that
S_{t}=I_{d}-(1-\alpha_{t})H_{t}+(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}H_{t}^{2}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3})
. This shows the validity of
(iii)
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}\left((\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}-\Sigma_{t}^{-1})\Sigma_{t}\right)=\mathrm{Tr}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}\Sigma_{t}-I_{d})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}\bigg(\left[I_{d}-(1-\alpha_{t})H_{t}+(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}H_{t}^{2}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad\left[I_{d}+(1-\alpha_{t})\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t})\right]-I_{d}\bigg)

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha_{t})\left[\mathrm{Tr}(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t}))-\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\left[\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t}^{2})-\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t}\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t}))\right]+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3}).

Adding this to the first term of (
F.2
) and taking expectation over
X_{t}\sim Q_{t}
(noting
5
here), we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1,t}}\Big[\left(\log(\det(\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}(X_{t})))-\log(\det(\Sigma_{t}(X_{t})))\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t}(X_{t}))^{\intercal}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}(X_{t})-\Sigma_{t}^{-1}(X_{t}))(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\Big]

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t}(X_{t})^{2})-2\mathrm{Tr}(H_{t}(X_{t})\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(X_{t}))+\mathrm{Tr}((\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(X_{t}))^{2})\right]

\displaystyle\qquad+\tilde{O}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iv)}}{{=}}\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{2}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left\lVert H_{t}(X_{t})-\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert_{F}^{2}+\tilde{O}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3}),

where
(iv)
follows because for two symmetric matrices
A
and
B
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Tr}(A^{2})-2\mathrm{Tr}(AB)+\mathrm{Tr}(B^{2})=\mathrm{Tr}(A^{2})-\mathrm{Tr}(AB)-\mathrm{Tr}(BA)+\mathrm{Tr}(B^{2})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Tr}((A-B)(A-B))=\mathrm{Tr}((A-B)^{\intercal}(A-B))=\left\lVert A-B\right\rVert_{F}^{2}.

Thus, following from
3
,

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1,t}}\Big[\left(\log(\det(\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}(X_{t})))-\log(\det(\Sigma_{t}(X_{t})))\right)\\
+(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t}(X_{t}))^{\intercal}(\widehat{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}(X_{t})-\Sigma_{t}^{-1}(X_{t}))(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\Big]\lesssim\frac{\log^{2}T}{T}\varepsilon_{H}^{2}.

(30)

Here
\varepsilon_{H}
is the Hessian estimation error. Combining (
F.2
) and (
30
) yields the desired result for the accelerated estimation error, which is in the order
\tilde{O}(1/T^{2})
.

F.3
Proof of
Corollary

2

Given the perturbed

\tilde{\Sigma}_{t}
in (
9
), following the definition in (
14
), we define,
\forall p\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{t}

\displaystyle:=(1-\alpha_{t})\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t})+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{4}(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t}))^{2}

\displaystyle\quad=(1-\alpha_{t})\left(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t})+\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{4}\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t})\right),

\displaystyle\tilde{B}_{t}

\displaystyle:=I_{d}-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\tilde{\Sigma}_{t}^{-1}=I_{d}-\tilde{A}_{t}+\tilde{A}_{t}^{2}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3})

\displaystyle\tilde{H}_{t}

\displaystyle:=H_{t}+\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{4}H_{t}.

Note that under
3
,

(1-\alpha_{t})\left\lVert\tilde{H}_{t}\right\rVert\lesssim(1-\alpha_{t})\left\lVert H_{t}\right\rVert+(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\left\lVert H_{t}\right\rVert^{2}=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{r}(Q_{t})}(1-\alpha_{t}),\penalty\ \forall r\geq 1.

Then, the rest of the proof
Lemma

4
still holds with
\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t})
and
H_{t}
replaced by
\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t})+\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{4}\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t})
and
\tilde{H}_{t}
. The proof is complete by noting that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left\lVert\tilde{H}_{t}(X_{t})-\left(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(X_{t})+\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{4}\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right)\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+(1-\alpha_{t}))\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left\lVert H_{t}(X_{t})-\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right\rVert_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\varepsilon_{H}^{2}.

F.4
Proof of
Lemma

5

By Bayes’ rule, for any
x_{t-1}
given fixed
x_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle q_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})

\displaystyle\propto q_{t-1}(x_{t-1})\exp\left(-\frac{\left\lVert x_{t}-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}x_{t-1}\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\alpha_{t})}\right)

\displaystyle\propto q_{t-1}(x_{t-1})p^{\prime}_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})\exp\left(\frac{1}{2}(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})^{\intercal}\Sigma_{t}^{-1}(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})-\frac{\left\lVert x_{t-1}-x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\alpha_{t})/\alpha_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle=q_{t-1}(x_{t-1})p^{\prime}_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})\exp\left(\frac{\alpha_{t}}{2(1-\alpha_{t})}(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})^{\intercal}(I_{d}-B_{t})(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})-\frac{\left\lVert x_{t-1}-x_{t}/\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\alpha_{t})/\alpha_{t}}\right)

(by
Equation

14
)

\displaystyle\propto p^{\prime}_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})\exp\left(\zeta_{t,t-1}(x_{t},x_{t-1})-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{2(1-\alpha_{t})}(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})^{\intercal}B_{t}(x_{t-1}-\mu_{t})\right),

where the last line follows from the definition of
\zeta_{t,t-1}(x_{t},x_{t-1})
in (
16
).
Now, with the definition of
\zeta^{\prime}_{t,t-1}(x_{t},x_{t-1})
in (
5
), we have

q_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})=\frac{p^{\prime}_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})e^{\zeta^{\prime}_{t,t-1}(x_{t},x_{t-1})}}{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}[e^{\zeta^{\prime}_{t,t-1}(x_{t},X_{t-1})}]}.

F.5
Proof of
Lemma

6

Recall
Equation

2
.
Let
\tilde{Q}_{0}
denote the distribution of
\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}
, and let
g(z)
denote the p.d.f. (w.r.t. the Lebesgue measure) of the distribution of
\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\bar{w}_{t}
. Note that
g
is a scaled version of the unit Gaussian p.d.f., and
\int_{z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}g(z)\mathrm{d}z=1<\infty
.
Now, for any event
A\subseteq\mathcal{B}(\lambda)
,

Q_{t}(A)=\int_{x\in A}\int_{\tilde{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}g(x-\tilde{x}_{0})\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(\tilde{x}_{0})\mathrm{d}x=\int_{\tilde{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left(\int_{x\in A}g(x-\tilde{x}_{0})\mathrm{d}x\right)\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(\tilde{x}_{0})

by Fubini’s theorem. If
A
has Lebesgue measure 0, by continuity of
g(x)
we get
\int_{x\in A}g(x-\tilde{x}_{0})\mathrm{d}x=0
, and thus
Q_{t}(A)=0
. This shows that
Q_{t}
is absolutely continuous w.r.t. the Lebesgue measure, and its p.d.f. exists, denoted as
q_{t}
.

Now, since any order of derivative of the Gaussian p.d.f. is bounded away from infinity and
\tilde{Q}_{0}
is a probability measure, we can invoke the dominated convergence theorem here to change the order of derivative and integral as

\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}q_{t}(x)=\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}\int_{\tilde{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}g(x-\tilde{x}_{0})\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(\tilde{x}_{0})=\int_{\tilde{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}g(x-\tilde{x}_{0})\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(\tilde{x}_{0}).

(31)

Thus, for any
k\geq 1
and any vector of indices
\bm{a}\in[d]^{k}
, we have

\left|\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}q_{t}(x)\right|\leq\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}g(x)\right|\int_{\tilde{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(\tilde{x}_{0})=\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left|\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}g(x)\right|<\infty.

This also implies that the Taylor term
\left|T_{k}(q_{t},x,\mu)\right|<\infty
for any
x
and
\mu
, and

\displaystyle q_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\tilde{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}g(x-\tilde{x}_{0})\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(\tilde{x}_{0})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\int_{\tilde{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lim_{p\to\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{p}T_{k}(g(x-\tilde{x}_{0}),x,\mu)\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(\tilde{x}_{0})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{=}}\lim_{p\to\infty}\int_{\tilde{x}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\sum_{k=0}^{p}T_{k}(g(x-\tilde{x}_{0}),x,\mu)\mathrm{d}\tilde{Q}_{0}(\tilde{x}_{0})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{=}}\lim_{p\to\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{p}T_{k}(q_{t},x,\mu)

where
(i)
follows because (scaled) Gaussian density is analytic,
(ii)
follows from dominated convergence theorem and the fact that
g
is a Gaussian density and has an upper bound independent of
\tilde{x}_{0}
, and
(iii)
follows from (
31
). This shows that
q_{t}
is analytic.

Finally, since
\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}\log q_{t}
is a smooth function of
q_{t},\partial^{1}q_{t},\dots,\partial^{k}q_{t}
, we have
\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}\log q_{t}(x_{t})<\infty
(possibly depending on
T
) for all
k\geq 1
and fixed (finite)
x_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Also,
\log q_{t}
is analytic because
\log(\cdot)
is analytic and
q_{t}(x_{t})>0,\penalty\ \forall x_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

F.6
Proof of
Lemma

7

The result follows directly from Isserlis’s Theorem, which says that

\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}\right]=\sum_{p\in P_{n}^{2}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in p}\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}Z_{j}]=\sum_{p\in P_{n}^{2}}\prod_{\{i,j\}\in p}\mathrm{Cov}(Z_{i},Z_{j})

since each
Z_{i}
is centered. Here
P_{n}^{2}
is the set that contains all distinct size-2 partitions of
[n]
. For example,
P_{4}^{2}=\{(\{1,2\},\{3,4\}),(\{1,3\},\{2,4\}),(\{1,4\},\{2,3\})\}
. Thus, since
A_{t}=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q)}(1-\alpha_{t})
under
5
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=0,\penalty\ \text{if $n$ is odd}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{n}Z_{i}\right]

\displaystyle=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}\left(\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{\frac{n}{2}}\right)=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{\frac{n}{2}}\right),\penalty\ \text{if $n$ is even}.

More specifically, following from Isserlis’s Theorem, the fourth moment is

\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{k}Z_{l}]=\mathrm{Cov}(Z_{i},Z_{j})\mathrm{Cov}(Z_{k},Z_{l})+\\
\mathrm{Cov}(Z_{i},Z_{k})\mathrm{Cov}(Z_{j},Z_{l})+\mathrm{Cov}(Z_{i},Z_{l})\mathrm{Cov}(Z_{j},Z_{k}),\penalty\ \forall i,j,k,l\in[d].

Here
\mathrm{Cov}(Z_{i},Z_{j})=\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}(\mathds{1}\left\{i=j\right\}+(1-\alpha_{t})A^{ij})
. The fourth moment result follows immediately by plugging into the formula. Turning to the sixth moment, we note that we are interested only in the coefficients for the terms that grow at a rate
\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3})
. Since the sixth moment consists of sum of product terms in which three covariance matrices are multiplied (giving us a rate at least
\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3})
), at least one product term in the sum must take covariance values only on the diagonal of the matrix. Therefore, only
\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}^{6}]
,
\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}^{4}Z_{j}^{2}]
, and
\mathbb{E}[Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}Z_{k}^{2}]
with
i,j,k
all differ satisfy this requirement, and we immediately get the desired result from Isserlis’s Theorem.

F.7
Proof of
Lemma

8

We first fix
x_{t}
and will take expectation at the end. Note that
q_{t|t-1}(x_{t}|x_{t-1})=\frac{1}{(2\pi(1-\alpha_{t}))^{d/2}}\exp\left(-\frac{\left\lVert x_{t}-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}x_{t-1}\right\rVert^{2}}{2(1-\alpha_{t})}\right)
. Following from the idea of Tweedie
[
31
]
, we have

\displaystyle q_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{q_{t-1}(x_{t-1})}{q_{t}(x_{t})}q_{t|t-1}(x_{t}|x_{t-1})

\displaystyle=\frac{q_{t-1}(x_{t-1})}{q_{t}(x_{t})}q_{t|t-1}(x_{t}|0)\exp\left(\frac{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}{1-\alpha_{t}}x_{t}^{\intercal}x_{t-1}-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{2(1-\alpha_{t})}\left\lVert x_{t-1}\right\rVert^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(q_{t-1}(x_{t-1})e^{-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{2(1-\alpha_{t})}\left\lVert x_{t-1}\right\rVert^{2}}\right)\exp\left(\frac{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}{1-\alpha_{t}}x_{t}^{\intercal}x_{t-1}-\log q_{t}(x_{t})+\log q_{t|t-1}(x_{t}|0)\right)

\displaystyle=:f(x_{t-1})\exp\left(x_{t-1}^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})\right)

(32)

where we have used the definitions of
\tilde{x}_{t}
and
\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})
in (
23
). This shows that
x_{t-1}
is a conditional exponential family given
\tilde{x}_{t}
.
Thus, the first moment can be found as (cf. Prop. 11.1 in
[
59
]
)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\tilde{x}_{t}}\int q_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}=\nabla_{\tilde{x}_{t}}\int f(x_{t-1})\exp\left(x_{t-1}^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})\right)\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}

\displaystyle=\int f(x_{t-1})\nabla_{\tilde{x}_{t}}\exp\left(x_{t-1}^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})\right)\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}

\displaystyle=\int f(x_{t-1})\exp\left(x_{t-1}^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})\right)\left(x_{t-1}-\nabla_{\tilde{x}_{t}}\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})\right)\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}

\displaystyle=\int f(x_{t-1})\exp\left(x_{t-1}^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})\right)x_{t-1}\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}-\nabla_{\tilde{x}_{t}}\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})

which implies that

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[X_{t-1}\right]=\nabla\kappa.

(33)

For the second moment,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\partial^{2}_{ij}\int q_{t-1|t}(x_{t-1}|x_{t})\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}

\displaystyle=\int f(x_{t-1})\frac{\partial}{\partial\tilde{x}_{t}^{j}}\Big(\exp\left(x_{t-1}^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})\right)\left(x_{t-1}^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})\right)\Big)\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}

\displaystyle=\int f(x_{t-1})\exp\left(x_{t-1}^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})\right)\left((x_{t-1}^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t}))(x_{t-1}^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t}))-\partial^{2}_{ij}\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})\right)\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}

which yields

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t})(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t})^{\intercal}\right]=\nabla^{2}\kappa=\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}I_{d}+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{\alpha_{t}}\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t}).

(34)

Below, we write
x=x_{t-1}
and
\kappa=\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})
for brevity. We remind readers that all derivatives are w.r.t.
\tilde{x}_{t}
instead of
x=x_{t-1}
. For the third moment,

0=\partial^{3}_{ijk}\int q_{t-1|t}\mathrm{d}x=:\int f(x)\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)D_{3}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})\mathrm{d}x

where

\displaystyle D_{3}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}+\kappa\right)\partial_{k}\Big(\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)\left((x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)-\partial^{2}_{ij}\kappa\right)\Big)

\displaystyle=(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)\left((x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)-\partial^{2}_{ij}\kappa\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+(-\partial^{2}_{ik}\kappa)(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)+(-\partial^{2}_{jk}\kappa)(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)-\partial^{3}_{ijk}\kappa.

(35)

Now, for any function
\mathrm{fn}(\tilde{x}_{t})
and
1\leq i\leq d
,

\int f(x)\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)\mathrm{fn}(\tilde{x}_{t})(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)\mathrm{d}x=0

by the first moment result (
33
). Thus, we get

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}^{i}-\mu_{t}^{i})(X_{t-1}^{j}-\mu_{t}^{j})(X_{t-1}^{k}-\mu_{t}^{k})\right]=\partial^{3}_{ijk}\kappa,

and by
5
,
\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}[\partial^{3}_{ijk}\kappa]=\tilde{O}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3})
.

For the fourth moment, we have

0=\partial^{4}_{ijkl}\int q_{t-1|t}\mathrm{d}x=:\int f(x)\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)D_{4}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})\mathrm{d}x

where

\displaystyle D_{4}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}+\kappa\right)\partial_{l}\Big(\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)((x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)

\displaystyle\qquad-\partial^{2}_{ij}\kappa(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)-\partial^{2}_{ik}\kappa(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)-\partial^{2}_{jk}\kappa(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)-\partial^{3}_{ijk}\kappa)\Big)

\displaystyle=(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)(x^{l}-\partial_{l}\kappa)+\partial_{l}\Big((x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad-\partial^{2}_{ij}\kappa(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)(x^{l}-\partial_{l}\kappa)-\partial^{3}_{ijl}\kappa(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)+\partial^{2}_{ij}\kappa\partial^{2}_{kl}\kappa

\displaystyle\qquad-\partial^{2}_{ik}\kappa(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)(x^{l}-\partial_{l}\kappa)-\partial^{3}_{ikl}\kappa(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)+\partial^{2}_{ik}\kappa\partial^{2}_{jl}\kappa

\displaystyle\qquad-\partial^{2}_{jk}\kappa(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{l}-\partial_{l}\kappa)-\partial^{3}_{jkl}\kappa(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)+\partial^{2}_{jk}\kappa\partial^{2}_{il}\kappa

\displaystyle\qquad-\partial^{3}_{ijk}\kappa(x^{l}-\partial_{l}\kappa)-\partial^{4}_{ijkl}\kappa

(36)

and

\displaystyle\partial_{l}\Big((x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)\Big)

\displaystyle=-\partial^{2}_{il}\kappa(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)-\partial^{2}_{jl}\kappa(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)-\partial^{2}_{kl}\kappa(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa).

Using the first and second moment results in (
33
) and (
34
), we get

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}^{i}-\mu_{t}^{i})(X_{t-1}^{j}-\mu_{t}^{j})(X_{t-1}^{k}-\mu_{t}^{k})(X_{t-1}^{l}-\mu_{t}^{l})\right]=\\
(\partial^{2}_{ij}\kappa)(\partial^{2}_{kl}\kappa)+(\partial^{2}_{ik}\kappa)(\partial^{2}_{jl}\kappa)+(\partial^{2}_{il}\kappa)(\partial^{2}_{jk}\kappa)+\partial^{4}_{ijkl}\kappa.

And the fourth moment result follows directly by applying (
34
) to each of the terms and taking the expectation over
X_{t}\sim Q_{t}
. The rate follows from
5
(cf.
Definition

2
).

F.8
Proof of
Lemma

9

The proof continues the idea of
Lemma

8
. The idea is to use the inductive relationship (provided in the proof of
Lemmas

8
and
10
):

\displaystyle D_{5}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}+\kappa\right)\partial_{m}\Big(\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)D_{4}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})\Big)

\displaystyle=(x^{m}-\partial_{m}\kappa)D_{4}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})+\partial_{m}D_{4}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})

\displaystyle D_{6}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}+\kappa\right)\partial_{n}\Big(\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)D_{5}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})\Big)

\displaystyle=(x^{n}-\partial_{n}\kappa)D_{5}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})+\partial_{n}D_{5}(x,\tilde{x}_{t}).

Let
P_{\ell}^{k}
be the set that contains all distinct size-
k
partitions of
[\ell]
. We use the definitions:

\displaystyle\binom{A}{k}

\displaystyle:=\Big\{\{a_{1},\dots,a_{k}\}:a_{1},\dots,a_{k}\in A,\penalty\ a_{1},\dots,a_{k}\penalty\ \text{all differ}\Big\},\penalty\ k\leq\left|A\right|

\displaystyle\mathrm{part}_{k}(A)

\displaystyle:=\{((a_{i},a_{j}):\{i,j\}\in p):p\in P_{\left|A\right|}^{k}\}.

Recall the formula for
D_{4}
in (
F.7
), which can be abbreviated as (here
\left|\bm{a}\right|=4
):

\displaystyle D_{4}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in\bm{a}}(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)-\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\prod_{i\in\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)+\sum_{(\bm{b},\bm{c})\in\mathrm{part}_{2}(\bm{a})}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{c}}\kappa

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{i\in\bm{a}}\partial^{3}_{\bm{a}\setminus\{i\}}\kappa(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)-\partial^{4}_{\bm{a}}\kappa.

Also recall the definition of
f(x)
in
Lemma

8
and that
\int f(x)e^{x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa}D_{p}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})\mathrm{d}x=0
, through which we can find the expected
p
-th moments of
\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[\prod_{i\in\bm{a}}(X^{i}_{t-1}-\mu^{i}_{t})\right]
. For reference, the first four moments are

\displaystyle\int f(x)\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)\mathrm{d}x=0

\displaystyle\int f(x)\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)\mathrm{d}x=\partial^{2}_{ij}\kappa=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}(1-\alpha_{t})

\displaystyle\int f(x)\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)\mathrm{d}x=\partial^{3}_{ijk}\kappa=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3})

\displaystyle\int f(x)\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)(x^{l}-\partial_{l}\kappa)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\qquad=(\partial^{2}_{ij}\kappa)(\partial^{2}_{kl}\kappa)+(\partial^{2}_{ik}\kappa)(\partial^{2}_{jl}\kappa)+(\partial^{2}_{il}\kappa)(\partial^{2}_{jk}\kappa)+\partial^{4}_{ijkl}\kappa=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{2})

where we note that
\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}\kappa=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{k})
for all
k\geq 3
.

We can calculate
D_{5}
as (with
\left|\bm{a}\right|=5
):

\displaystyle D_{5}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=(x^{a_{5}}-\partial_{a_{5}}\kappa)D_{4}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})+\partial_{a_{5}}D_{4}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in\bm{a}}(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)-\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\prod_{i\in\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)-\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}}\partial^{3}_{\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}\kappa\prod_{i\in\bm{b}}(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in\bm{a}\\
(\bm{b},\bm{c})\in\mathrm{part}_{2}(\bm{a}\setminus\{i\})\end{subarray}}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{c}}\kappa(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{i\in\bm{a}}\partial^{4}_{\bm{a}\setminus\{i\}}\kappa(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)+\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{3}_{\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}\kappa-\partial^{5}_{\bm{a}}\kappa.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[\prod_{i\in\bm{a}:\left|\bm{a}\right|=5}(X_{t-1}^{i}-\mu_{t}^{i})\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{3}_{\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}\kappa+\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}}\partial^{3}_{\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa-\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{3}_{\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}\kappa+\partial^{5}_{\bm{a}}\kappa

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{3}_{\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}\kappa+\partial^{5}_{\bm{a}}\kappa=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}).

Now we turn to calculate
D_{6}
(and let
\left|\bm{a}\right|=6
):

\displaystyle D_{6}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})=(x^{a_{6}}-\partial_{a_{6}}\kappa)D_{5}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})+\partial_{a_{6}}D_{5}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=\prod_{i\in\bm{a}}(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)-\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\prod_{i\in\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)-\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{3}}\partial^{3}_{\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}\kappa\prod_{i\in\bm{b}}(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}}\partial^{4}_{\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}\kappa\prod_{i\in\bm{b}}(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}\\
(\bm{c},\bm{e})\in\mathrm{part}_{2}(\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b})\end{subarray}}\partial^{2}_{\bm{c}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{e}}\kappa\prod_{i\in\bm{b}}(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)+\sum_{i\in\bm{a}}\mathrm{fn}(\kappa)(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{(\bm{b},\bm{c},\bm{e})\in\mathrm{part}_{2}(\bm{a})}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{c}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{e}}\kappa+\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{4}_{\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}\kappa+\sum_{(\bm{b},\bm{c})\in\mathrm{part}_{3}(\bm{a})}\partial^{3}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{3}_{\bm{c}}\kappa-\partial^{6}_{\bm{a}}\kappa.

Here
\mathrm{fn}(\kappa)
is a function of
\kappa
which does not depend on
x
. Note that
\mathrm{fn}
does not affect the expected value because
\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}[X_{t-1}-\mu_{t}]=0
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[\prod_{i\in\bm{a}:\left|\bm{a}\right|=6}(X_{t-1}^{i}-\mu_{t}^{i})\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\left(\sum_{(\bm{c},\bm{e})\in\mathrm{part}_{2}(\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b})}\partial^{2}_{\bm{c}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{e}}\kappa+\partial^{4}_{\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}\kappa\right)+\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{3}}\partial^{3}_{\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{3}_{\bm{b}}\kappa

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}}\partial^{4}_{\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}\\
(\bm{c},\bm{e})\in\mathrm{part}_{2}(\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b})\end{subarray}}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{c}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{e}}\kappa

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{(\bm{b},\bm{c},\bm{e})\in\mathrm{part}_{2}(\bm{a})}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{c}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{e}}\kappa-\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{4}_{\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}\kappa-\sum_{(\bm{b},\bm{c})\in\mathrm{part}_{3}(\bm{a})}\partial^{3}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{3}_{\bm{c}}\kappa+\partial^{6}_{\bm{a}}\kappa

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{b}\in\binom{\bm{a}}{2}}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{4}_{\bm{a}\setminus\bm{b}}\kappa+\sum_{(\bm{b},\bm{c})\in\mathrm{part}_{3}(\bm{a})}\partial^{3}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{3}_{\bm{c}}\kappa+\sum_{(\bm{b},\bm{c},\bm{e})\in\mathrm{part}_{2}(\bm{a})}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{c}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{e}}\kappa+\partial^{6}_{\bm{a}}\kappa

\displaystyle=\sum_{(\bm{b},\bm{c},\bm{e})\in\mathrm{part}_{2}(\bm{a})}\partial^{2}_{\bm{b}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{c}}\kappa\partial^{2}_{\bm{e}}\kappa+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{5}).

The proof is now complete.

F.9
Proof of
Lemma

10

We fix
x_{t}
first and will take the expectation at the end. We first introduce some notations used in the proof. We write
x=x_{t-1}
and
\kappa=\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})
. Given a set of indices
A
, define its bipartition as

\mathrm{bipart}(A):=\{(B,C):A=B\sqcup C\}

where
B
and
C
are both
sets
of indices (and therefore the order of indices within each of
B
and
C
does not matter). Here
\sqcup
refers to the
disjoint
union of the two sets (which is only defined when the two sets are disjoint). Next, given a set
B
, define
\mathrm{allpart_{\geq 2}}(B)
as a set containing all partitions of
B
such that there are
at least
2 elements in each part of the partition. As an example,
\mathrm{allpart_{\geq 2}(\{1,2,3,4\})}=\{\{\{1,2\},\{3,4\}\},\{\{1,3\},\{2,4\}\},\{\{1,4\},\{2,3\}\}
, and
\{\{1\},\{2,3,4\}\}\notin\mathrm{allpart_{\geq 2}(\{1,2,3,4\})}
despite the fact that it is a valid partition.
For each partition
b\in\mathrm{allpart_{\geq 2}}(B)
, define

\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa:=\prod_{\xi\in b}\partial^{\left|\xi\right|}_{a_{\xi}}\kappa.

Here note that
\xi
is also a set, and
\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa
is well defined since the order of indices to take partial derivative with does not matter.
Define

\displaystyle D_{0}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})

\displaystyle:=1

\displaystyle D_{p}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})

\displaystyle:=\exp\left(-x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}+\kappa\right)\partial_{a_{p}}\Big(\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)D_{p-1}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})\Big)

for all
p\geq 1
.
We again remind readers that all derivatives are w.r.t.
\tilde{x}_{t}
instead of
x=x_{t-1}
.

By working out the derivative, a direct implication of the definition of
D_{p}
is a recursive relationship:

D_{p}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})=(x^{a_{p}}-\partial_{a_{p}}\kappa)D_{p-1}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})+\partial_{a_{p}}D_{p-1}(x,\tilde{x}_{t}).

Also, if we unroll the recursion of
D_{p}
, we get

\displaystyle D_{p}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}+\kappa\right)\partial_{a_{p}}\Big(\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)D_{p-1}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})\Big)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}+\kappa\right)\partial_{a_{p}}\Big(\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)\exp\left(-x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}+\kappa\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\partial_{a_{p-1}}\Big(\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)D_{p-2}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})\Big)\Big)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}+\kappa\right)\partial^{2}_{a_{p},a_{p-1}}\Big(\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)D_{p-2}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})\Big)

\displaystyle=\exp\left(-x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}+\kappa\right)\partial^{p}_{a_{p},\dots,a_{1}}\Big(\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)\Big)

and thus

\displaystyle 0=\partial^{p}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{p}}\int q_{t-1|t}\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int f(x)\partial^{p}_{a_{1},\dots,a_{p}}\Big(\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)\Big)\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=\int f(x)\exp\left(x^{\intercal}\tilde{x}_{t}-\kappa\right)D_{p}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})\mathrm{d}x

(37)

where we recall the definition of
f(x)
back in (
F.7
).

In the following, we present the entire proof into two parts. In part 1, we inductively show that each
D_{p}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})
satisfies a particular polynomial form. In part 2, we inductively show that this polynomial form results in the desired rates.

Part 1 of the proof of Lemma
10
:

The first step toward proving the desired results is to obtain the form of
D_{p}
for all
p\geq 2
. Now, we aim to show inductively that

D_{p}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})=\prod_{i=1}^{p}(x^{a_{i}}-\partial_{a_{i}}\kappa)-\sum_{(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([p])}\sum_{b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)}d_{p}(b,C)(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\prod_{c\in C}(x^{a_{c}}-\partial_{a_{c}}\kappa)

(38)

where
d_{p}(b,C)
is a constant from combinatorics, which is possibly 0 and which only depends on
p
. From Lemma
8
, the bases cases have been established that (cf. (
F.7
) and (
F.7
))

\displaystyle D_{2}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})=(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)-\partial^{2}_{ij}\kappa

\displaystyle D_{3}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})=(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)

\displaystyle\quad-\partial^{2}_{ij}\kappa(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)-\partial^{2}_{ik}\kappa(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)-\partial^{2}_{jk}\kappa(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)-\partial^{3}_{ijk}\kappa

\displaystyle D_{4}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})=(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)(x^{l}-\partial_{l}\kappa)

\displaystyle\quad-\partial^{2}_{ij}\kappa(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)(x^{l}-\partial_{l}\kappa)-\partial^{2}_{ik}\kappa(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)(x^{l}-\partial_{l}\kappa)-\partial^{2}_{jk}\kappa(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{l}-\partial_{l}\kappa)

\displaystyle\quad+\partial_{l}((x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa))-\partial^{3}_{ijk}\kappa(x^{l}-\partial_{l}\kappa)-\partial^{3}_{ijl}(x^{k}-\partial_{k}\kappa)

\displaystyle\quad-\partial^{3}_{ikl}(x^{j}-\partial_{j}\kappa)-\partial^{3}_{jkl}(x^{i}-\partial_{i}\kappa)+\partial^{2}_{ij}\kappa\partial^{2}_{kl}\kappa+\partial^{2}_{ik}\kappa\partial^{2}_{jl}\kappa+\partial^{2}_{jk}\kappa\partial^{2}_{il}\kappa-\partial^{4}_{ijkl}\kappa.

In particular, each term of
D_{p}
(
p=2,3,4
) is in the form of either
\prod_{i=1}^{p}(x^{a_{i}}-\partial_{a_{i}}\kappa)
or
(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\prod_{c\in C}(x^{a_{c}}-\partial_{a_{c}}\kappa)
, where
\left|\xi\right|\geq 2,\penalty\ \forall\xi\in b
, and
(\sqcup_{\xi\in b}\xi)\sqcup C=[p]
. Therefore,
D_{2},D_{3},D_{4}
all satisfy the hypothesis (
38
).

Turning to the inductive step, we suppose that
D_{k}
satisfies (
38
), i.e.,

D_{k}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})=\prod_{i=1}^{k}(x^{a_{i}}-\partial_{a_{i}}\kappa)-\sum_{(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k])}\sum_{b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)}d_{k}(b,C)(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\prod_{c\in C}(x^{a_{c}}-\partial_{a_{c}}\kappa).

Then, using the recursive relationship, we have

\displaystyle D_{k+1}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=(x^{a_{k+1}}-\partial_{a_{k+1}}\kappa)D_{k}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})+\partial_{a_{k+1}}D_{k}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\prod_{i=1}^{k+1}(x^{a_{i}}-\partial_{a_{i}}\kappa)}_{T_{1}}-\underbrace{\sum_{(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k])}\sum_{b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)}d_{k}(b,C)(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\prod_{c\in C}(x^{a_{c}}-\partial_{a_{c}}\kappa)(x^{a_{k+1}}-\partial_{a_{k+1}}\kappa)}_{T_{2}}

\displaystyle\quad-\underbrace{\partial_{a_{k+1}}\left(-\prod_{i=1}^{k}(x^{a_{i}}-\partial_{a_{i}}\kappa)\right)}_{T_{3}}-\underbrace{\sum_{(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k])}\sum_{b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)}d_{k}(b,C)(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\left(\partial_{a_{k+1}}\prod_{c\in C}(x^{a_{c}}-\partial_{a_{c}}\kappa)\right)}_{T_{4}}

\displaystyle\quad-\underbrace{\sum_{(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k])}\sum_{b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)}d_{k}(b,C)\left(\partial_{a_{k+1}}(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\right)\prod_{c\in C}(x^{a_{c}}-\partial_{a_{c}}\kappa)}_{T_{5}}

\displaystyle=T_{1}-T_{2}-T_{3}-T_{4}-T_{5}

where we define each term as
T_{1},\dots,T_{5}
.
Now we discuss these terms separately:

1.

T_{1}
(and only
T_{1}
) is in the form
\prod_{i=1}^{k+1}(x^{a_{i}}-\partial_{a_{i}}\kappa)
.

2.

T_{2}
is a summation of individual terms:
(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\prod_{c\in C}(x^{a_{c}}-\partial_{a_{c}}\kappa)(x^{a_{k+1}}-\partial_{a_{k+1}}\kappa)
. Here
b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)
and
(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k])
. Thus, by definition of
\mathrm{bipart}
and
\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}
, for each
\xi\in b
,
\left|\xi\right|\geq 2
and
(\sqcup_{\xi\in b}\xi)\sqcup C=[k]
. Therefore,
k+1\notin B\sqcup C
and

(\sqcup_{\xi\in b}\xi)\sqcup C\sqcup\{k+1\}=[k]\sqcup\{k+1\}=[k+1].

This implies that each individual term of
T_{2}
is in the form of
(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\prod_{c\in C_{2}}(x^{c}-\partial_{c}\kappa)
where
b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B_{2})
, such that
B_{2}:=B
and
C_{2}:=C\sqcup\{k+1\}
. Here
C_{2}
is well defined because
k+1\notin C
. Since
(B_{2},C_{2})\in\mathrm{bipart}([k+1])
,

T_{2}=\sum_{(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k+1])}\sum_{b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)}d_{2}(b,C)(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\prod_{c\in C}(x^{a_{c}}-\partial_{a_{c}}\kappa)

for some constant
d_{2}(b,C)
.

3.

T_{3}
is the derivative of product, which is a summation of individual terms:
(\partial^{2}_{a_{j},a_{k+1}}\kappa)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i=1\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}^{d}(x^{a_{i}}-\partial_{a_{i}}\kappa),\penalty\ j=1,\dots,k
. Therefore, for each
j=1,\dots,k
, each term is of the form
(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\prod_{c\in C_{3}}(x^{a_{c}}-\partial_{a_{c}}\kappa)
where
b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B_{3})
, such that
B_{3}:=\{j,k+1\}
and
C_{3}:=[k]\setminus\{j\}
. Since
(B_{3},C_{3})\in\mathrm{bipart}([k+1])
,

T_{3}=\sum_{(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k+1])}\sum_{b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)}d_{3}(b,C)(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\prod_{c\in C}(x^{a_{c}}-\partial_{a_{c}}\kappa)

for some constant
d_{3}(b,C)
.

4.

T_{4}
is a summation of individual terms:
(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\left(\partial_{a_{k+1}}\prod_{c\in C}(x^{a_{c}}-\partial_{a_{c}}\kappa)\right)
where
b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)
and
(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k])
. Now,

\displaystyle(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\left(\partial_{a_{k+1}}\prod_{c\in C}(x^{a_{c}}-\partial_{a_{c}}\kappa)\right)

\displaystyle=-(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)(\partial^{2}_{a_{j},a_{k+1}}\kappa)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in C\\
i\neq c\end{subarray}}(x^{a_{i}}-\partial_{a_{i}}\kappa)

\displaystyle=-(\bm{\partial}_{b_{4}}\kappa)\prod_{i\in C_{4}}(x^{a_{i}}-\partial_{a_{i}}\kappa)

where
b_{4}:=b\sqcup\{k+1,c\}
and
C_{4}:=C\setminus\{c\}
. Here
b_{4}
is well defined because
k+1,c\notin b
. Define
B_{4}:=[k+1]\setminus C_{4}
, and we have
b_{4}\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B_{4})
. Since
(B_{4},C_{4})
is a valid partition of
[k+1]
, we have

T_{4}=\sum_{(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k+1])}\sum_{b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)}d_{4}(b,C)(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\prod_{c\in C}(x^{a_{c}}-\partial_{a_{c}}\kappa)

for some constant
d_{4}(b,C)
.

5.

T_{5}
is a summation of individual terms:
\left(\partial_{a_{k+1}}(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\right)\prod_{c\in C}(x^{a_{c}}-\partial_{a_{c}}\kappa)
, where
b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)
and
(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k])
. From definition of
\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa
,

\partial_{a_{k+1}}(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)=\partial_{a_{k+1}}\left(\prod_{\xi\in b}\partial^{\left|\xi\right|}_{a_{\xi}}\kappa\right)=\sum_{\xi\in b}\left(\partial^{\left|\xi\right|+1}_{a_{\xi},a_{k+1}}\kappa\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}\zeta\in b\\
\zeta\neq\xi\end{subarray}}\partial^{\left|\zeta\right|}_{a_{\zeta}}\kappa=\sum_{\xi\in b}\bm{\partial}_{b_{\xi}}\kappa

where, for each
\xi\in b
, we have defined a new partition
b_{\xi}
such that
k+1
is added to the
\xi
in the partition
b
. Formally, define
b_{\xi}:=b\setminus\xi\sqcup\{\xi\sqcup\{k+1\}\}
, which is well defined because
\xi\notin(b\setminus\xi)
and
k+1\notin B
. Define
B_{5}:=B\sqcup\{k+1\}
and
C_{5}:=C
, and note that
(B_{5},C_{5})
is a valid partition of
[k+1]
. Since
\left|\zeta\right|\geq 2,\penalty\ \forall\zeta\in b
, we have
\left|\zeta^{\prime}\right|\geq 2,\penalty\ \forall\zeta^{\prime}\in b_{\xi}
. Since
b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)
, we have
b_{\xi}\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B_{5})
for all
\xi\in b
. Therefore, for any fixed
C(=C_{5})

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)}d_{k}(b,C)\left(\partial_{a_{k+1}}(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)}\sum_{\xi\in b}d_{k}(b,C)\bm{\partial}_{b_{\xi}}\kappa

\displaystyle=\sum_{b_{5}\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B_{5})}d_{5}(b_{5},C)\bm{\partial}_{b_{5}}\kappa

for some constant
d_{5}(b_{5},C)
, and thus

T_{5}=\sum_{(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k+1])}\sum_{b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)}d_{5}(b,C)(\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa)\prod_{c\in C}(x^{a_{c}}-\partial_{a_{c}}\kappa).

Finally, letting

d_{k+1}(b,C):=\sum_{j=2}^{5}d_{j}(b,C)

for each
b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)
and
C
such that
(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k+1])
, we have shown that if
D_{k}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})
is in the form of (
38
),
D_{k+1}(x,\tilde{x}_{t})
is also in this form. Thus, claim (
38
) is valid for all
p\geq 2
.

Part 2 of the proof of Lemma
10
:

First, we remind readers of the definition of
\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})
in (
23
). Also, the partial derivatives within the expectation over
X_{t}\sim Q_{t}
do not affect the rate by
5
. Note that
\nabla\kappa=\mu_{t}
from direct differentiation. From (
F.9
) and (
38
), for fixed
x_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{p}(X_{t-1}^{a_{i}}-\mu_{t}^{a_{i}})\right]

\displaystyle=\tilde{O}\left(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([p])\\
b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)\end{subarray}}\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[\prod_{c\in C}(X_{t-1}^{a_{c}}-\mu_{t}^{a_{c}})\right]\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{O}\left(\sup_{(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([p])}\left(\sup_{b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)}\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})\right)\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[\prod_{c\in C}(X_{t-1}^{a_{c}}-\mu_{t}^{a_{c}})\right]\right).

(39)

We first consider the term
\sup_{b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)}\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})
. Given a partition
b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)
, direct differentiation yields

\displaystyle\partial^{\left|\xi\right|}_{a_{\xi}}\kappa

\displaystyle=\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{\alpha_{t}}\partial^{2}_{a_{\xi}}\log q_{t}(x_{t})=\tilde{O}(1-\alpha_{t}),\quad\text{if }\left|\xi\right|=2\text{ and }\xi_{1}=\xi_{2}

\displaystyle\partial^{\left|\xi\right|}_{a_{\xi}}\kappa

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{\left|\xi\right|}}{\alpha_{t}^{\left|\xi\right|/2}}\partial^{\left|\xi\right|}_{\xi}\log q_{t}(x_{t})=\tilde{O}((1-\alpha_{t})^{\left|\xi\right|}),\quad\text{for all other }\xi.

Since by definition
\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa=\prod_{\xi\in b}\partial^{\left|\xi\right|}_{a_{\xi}}\kappa
and
\sqcup_{\xi\in b}\xi=B
, the slowest rate of
\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa
(as a function of
B
) is determined by the partition
b
containing the most number of equal pairs. The slowest rate is

\sup_{b\in\mathrm{allpart}_{\geq 2}(B)}\bm{\partial}_{b}\kappa(\tilde{x}_{t})=\begin{cases}\tilde{O}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{(\left|B\right|-1)/2}(1-\alpha_{t})^{3}\right)=\tilde{O}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{(\left|B\right|+5)/2}\right)&\text{if }\left|B\right|\text{is odd}\\
\tilde{O}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{\left|B\right|/2}\right)&\text{if }\left|B\right|\text{is even}\end{cases}

To proceed, we will again use induction to find the overall rate. From Lemma
8
, base cases have been established that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1,t}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{2}(X_{t-1}^{a_{i}}-\mu_{t}^{a_{i}})\right]

\displaystyle=\tilde{O}\left(1-\alpha_{t}\right),\penalty\ \forall a\in[d]^{2}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1,t}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{3}(X_{t-1}^{a_{i}}-\mu_{t}^{a_{i}})\right]

\displaystyle=\tilde{O}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{3}\right),\penalty\ \forall a\in[d]^{3}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1,t}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{4}(X_{t-1}^{a_{i}}-\mu_{t}^{a_{i}})\right]

\displaystyle=\tilde{O}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\right),\penalty\ \forall a\in[d]^{4}.

These rates satisfy (
24
) and (
25
) when
p=2,3,4
. Now we turn to the inductive step. Suppose
k\geq 4
is even. For purpose of induction, suppose (
24
) and (
25
) hold for all
p=2,\dots,k
. Then, following (
F.9
), for
p=k+1
(odd number), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1,t}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{k+1}(X_{t-1}^{a_{i}}-\mu_{t}^{a_{i}})\right]

\displaystyle=O\Bigg(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k+1])\\
\left|B\right|\text{ odd},\penalty\ \left|C\right|\text{ even}\end{subarray}}(1-\alpha_{t})^{(\left|B\right|+5)/2}(1-\alpha_{t})^{\left|C\right|/2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k+1])\\
\left|B\right|\text{ even},\penalty\ \left|C\right|\text{ odd}\end{subarray}}(1-\alpha_{t})^{\left|B\right|/2}(1-\alpha_{t})^{(\left|C\right|+3)/2}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=O\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{(k+1)/2+5/2}+(1-\alpha_{t})^{(k+1)/2+3/2}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{(k+1)/2+3/2}\right).

Then, for
p=k+2
(even number), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1,t}}\left[\prod_{i=1}^{k+2}(X_{t-1}^{a_{i}}-\mu_{t}^{a_{i}})\right]

\displaystyle=O\Bigg(\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k+2])\\
\left|B\right|\text{ odd},\penalty\ \left|C\right|\text{ odd}\end{subarray}}(1-\alpha_{t})^{(\left|B\right|+5)/2}(1-\alpha_{t})^{(\left|C\right|+3)/2}

\displaystyle\qquad+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}(B,C)\in\mathrm{bipart}([k+1])\\
\left|B\right|\text{ even},\penalty\ \left|C\right|\text{even}\end{subarray}}(1-\alpha_{t})^{\left|B\right|/2}(1-\alpha_{t})^{\left|C\right|/2}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=O\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{(k+2)/2+4}+(1-\alpha_{t})^{(k+2)/2}\right)

\displaystyle=O\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{(k+2)/2}\right).

These show the validity of the claims (
24
) and (
25
). The proof is now complete.

F.10
Proof of
Lemma

11

Before analyzing the rate of each moment, we need to guarantee the validity of exchanging the limit (in the Taylor expansion) and the expectation operator.
Intuitively, this is achievable under
5
, where the Taylor series is absolutely convergent in expectation due to its Gaussian-like moments.
Specifically, since
\log q_{t-1}
is analytic, all its partial derivatives exist. Following from the Taylor expansion of
\zeta^{\prime}_{t,t-1}
in (
D.3
),

\displaystyle\lim_{k\to\infty}\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{t}\sim Q_{t}\\
X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}\end{subarray}}[\zeta^{\prime}_{t,t-1}]-\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{t}\sim Q_{t}\\
X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}\end{subarray}}\bigg[T_{1}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})+T_{2}^{\prime}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{p=3}^{k}T_{p}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})\bigg]\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{t}\sim Q_{t}\\
X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}\end{subarray}}\Bigg|\zeta^{\prime}_{t,t-1}-T_{1}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})-T_{2}^{\prime}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{p=3}^{k}T_{p}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\lim_{k\to\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{t}\sim Q_{t}\\
X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}\end{subarray}}\left[\sum_{p=k+1}^{\infty}\left|T_{p}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\lim_{k\to\infty}\liminf_{\ell\to\infty}\sum_{p=k+1}^{\ell}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{t}\sim Q_{t}\\
X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}\end{subarray}}\left|T_{p}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})\right|

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{=}}0.

Here
(i)
follows from Fatou’s lemma, and
(ii)
is because, under
5
and
Lemma

7
, we have
\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{t}\sim Q_{t}\\
X_{t-1}\sim P_{t-1|t}\end{subarray}}|T_{p}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})|=\tilde{O}\left(T^{-p/2}\right)
, and thus
the infinite sum is convergent for all
(k,\ell)
such that
1\leq k<\ell<\infty
since

\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{t}\sim Q_{t}\\
X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}\end{subarray}}\left|T_{p}(\log q_{t-1},x_{t-1},\mu_{t})\right|=\tilde{O}\left(\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{p!}\cdot\frac{d^{p}}{T^{p/2}}\right)<\infty.

The proof for
\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{t}\sim Q_{t}\\
X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}\end{subarray}}
is similar due to its Gaussian-like concentration of all centralized moments (see
Lemma

10
). Thus, we are able to exchange the infinite sum and the expectation under either
P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}\times Q_{t}
or
Q_{t-1,t}
.

Next, we put together the rates of the conditional moments. We use abbreviated notations as
T_{p}=T_{p}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})
. To investigate the dominant term, we analyze the expected difference of the first 8 moments in the Taylor expansion (
D.3
) separately. First, for any fixed
x_{t}
,

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[T_{1}\right]=0=\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}\left[T_{1}\right].

Also, for
T^{\prime}_{2}
, note that for any random variable
Z
(regardless of its distribution) with
\mathbb{E}Z=0
and
\mathrm{Cov}(Z)=\Sigma
, the mean of the quadratic form (with fixed matrix
\Xi
) is

\mathbb{E}[Z^{\intercal}\Xi Z]=\mathbb{E}[\mathrm{Tr}\left(Z^{\intercal}\Xi Z\right)]=\mathrm{Tr}\left(\Xi\Sigma\right).

This implies that, for any fixed
x_{t}
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}[T^{\prime}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t})^{\intercal}\left(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{1-\alpha_{t}}B_{t}\right)(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{1-\alpha_{t}}B_{t}\right)\Sigma_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t})^{\intercal}\left(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{1-\alpha_{t}}B_{t}\right)(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}[T^{\prime}_{2}].

Using
Lemmas

8
and
7
, the rate for
T_{3}
is

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}\right)\left[T_{3}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1,t}}\left[T_{3}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{3}}{3!\alpha_{t}^{3/2}}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}[\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(X_{t})].

Using
Lemmas

10
and
7
, and when the partial derivatives satisfy
5
, the rate for
T_{5}
,
T_{7}
, and
T_{p}(p\geq 8)
can also be determined:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}\right)\left[T_{5}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1,t}}\left[T_{5}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\tilde{O}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}\right)\left[T_{7}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1,t}}\left[T_{7}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\tilde{O}((1-\alpha_{t})^{5}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}\right)\left[T_{p}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\quad=\tilde{O}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}),\penalty\ \forall p\geq 8.

The remaining orders are
T_{4}
and
T_{6}
. The following proof will draw from the results in
Lemmas

8
,
9
and
7
. Fix
p\geq 1
. Write
Z_{i}=X_{t-1}^{i}-\mu_{t}^{i}
and
A^{ij}=[A_{t}]^{ij}
for
i,j\in[d]
. For
T_{4}
, let
i,j,k,l\in[d]
all differ, and the difference (in expectation) of each term of
T_{4}
is

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}^{4}]-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}^{4}]

\displaystyle\qquad=3\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{2}+6\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{3}}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}\partial^{2}_{ii}\log q_{t}(x_{t})-3\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{2}(1+A^{ii})^{2}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=-3\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{2}(A^{ii})^{2}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}\right),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}^{3}Z_{j}]-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}^{3}Z_{j}]

\displaystyle\qquad=3\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{3}}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}\partial^{2}_{ij}\log q_{t}(x_{t})-3\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{2}A^{ij}(1+A^{ii})+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=-3\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{2}A^{ij}A^{ii}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}\right),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}]-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}]

\displaystyle\qquad=\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{2}+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{3}}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}\left(\partial^{2}_{ii}\log q_{t}(x_{t})+\partial^{2}_{jj}\log q_{t}(x_{t})\right)-\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{2}(1+A^{ii})(1+A^{jj})

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=-\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{2}A^{ii}A^{jj}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}\right),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}Z_{k}]-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}Z_{k}]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{3}}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}\partial^{2}_{jk}\log q_{t}(x_{t})-\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{2}(1+A^{ii})A^{jk}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad=-\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}}{\alpha_{t}^{2}}A^{ii}A^{jk}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}\right),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{k}Z_{l}]-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}Z_{j}Z_{k}Z_{l}]=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}\right).

Recall from (
14
) that
A_{t}=(1-\alpha_{t})\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t})=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}(1-\alpha_{t})
under
5
. Hence, many low-order terms above are cancelled, and we get

\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}\right)\left[T_{4}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})\right]=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}\right).

Now we turn to
T_{6}
. Let
i,j,k\in[d]
all differ, and the difference (in expectation) of each lowest-order term of
T_{6}
is

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}^{6}]-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}^{6}]

\displaystyle\qquad=15\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{3}-15\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{3}(1+A^{ii})^{3}+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}^{4}Z_{j}^{2}]-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}^{4}Z_{j}^{2}]

\displaystyle\qquad=3\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{3}-3\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{3}(1+A^{ii})^{2}(1+A^{jj})+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}),

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}Z_{k}^{2}]-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}[Z_{i}^{2}Z_{j}^{2}Z_{k}^{2}]

\displaystyle\qquad=\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{3}-\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}}\right)^{3}(1+A^{ii})(1+A^{jj})(1+A^{kk})+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}).

Also, by
Lemmas

7
and
9
, the rest of the terms already satisfy
\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4})
under
5
. The low-order terms cancel in the same way as for
T_{4}
, and thus,

\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}\right)\left[T_{6}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})\right]=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}).

Therefore, the lowest order term above is
T_{3}
, whose order is
\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{p}(Q_{t})}((1-\alpha_{t})^{3})
. The proof is now complete.

F.11
Proof of
Corollary

3

The proof is very similar to
Lemma

11
and (
D.3
), except with a perturbed covariance matrix. We employ the notations
\tilde{A}_{t}
and
\tilde{B}_{t}
from
Remark

3
. Here we have that
\tilde{A}_{t}(X_{t})=A_{t}(X_{t})+\Xi_{t}(X_{t})
, and thus,
\forall r\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\tilde{B}_{t}(X_{t})

\displaystyle=B_{t}(X_{t})+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{r}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\right)=A_{t}(X_{t})+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{r}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=(1-\alpha_{t})\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(X_{t})+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{r}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\right).

Compare with the proof of
Lemma

11
, the only difference is the expected difference of
T^{\prime}_{2}
. Since
\tilde{A}_{t}(X_{t})=A_{t}(X_{t})+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{r}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\right)
and
\tilde{B}_{t}(X_{t})=B_{t}(X_{t})+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{r}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\right)
, the expected differences of all higher order
T_{p}
’s have the same rate as the non-perturbed case.

Now, for any fixed
x_{t}
and
r\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}[T^{\prime}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t})^{\intercal}\left(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{1-\alpha_{t}}\tilde{B}_{t}\right)(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{1-\alpha_{t}}\tilde{B}_{t}\right)\tilde{\Sigma}_{t}\right),

and, from
Lemma

8
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}[T^{\prime}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}\left[(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t})^{\intercal}\left(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{1-\alpha_{t}}\tilde{B}_{t}\right)(X_{t-1}-\mu_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{1-\alpha_{t}}\tilde{B}_{t}\right)\Sigma_{t}\right).

Thus,

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}\right)\left[T^{\prime}_{2}(\log q_{t-1},X_{t-1},\mu_{t})\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{1-\alpha_{t}}\tilde{B}_{t}\right)\left(\Sigma_{t}-\tilde{\Sigma}_{t}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{2\alpha_{t}}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})-\frac{\alpha_{t}}{1-\alpha_{t}}\tilde{B}_{t}\right)\Xi_{t}\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{2\alpha_{t}}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})-\alpha_{t}\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right)\Xi_{t}\right)+\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{r}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}\right).

Note that here the first term is in the order
\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{r}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{3}\right)
under
5
since
\Xi_{t}(X_{t})=\tilde{O}_{\mathcal{L}^{r}(Q_{t})}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\right)
.
Therefore, under the perturbed case,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim Q_{t-1|t}}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1}\sim P^{\prime}_{t-1|t}}\right)[\zeta^{\prime}_{t,t-1}]

\displaystyle\qquad=-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{2\alpha_{t}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\mathrm{Tr}\left(\left(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))-\alpha_{t}\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right)\Xi_{t}(X_{t})\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{3}}{3!\alpha_{t}^{3/2}}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}[\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(X_{t})]

\displaystyle\qquad+\tilde{O}((1-\alpha_{t})^{4}).

The final result can be achieved using (
D.3
). The proof is complete.

F.12
Proof of
Lemma

12

From (
1
), the forward process at the first step is

x_{1}=\sqrt{\alpha_{1}}x_{0}+\sqrt{1-\alpha_{1}}w_{1}

where
w_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
is independent of
Q_{0}
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}\sim Q_{1},X_{0}\sim Q_{0}}\left\lVert X_{1}-X_{0}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{W_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d}),X_{0}\sim Q_{0}}\left\lVert\sqrt{1-\alpha_{1}}W_{1}+(\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}-1)X_{0}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\mathbb{E}_{W_{1}\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})}\left\lVert\sqrt{1-\alpha_{1}}W_{1}\right\rVert^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0}}\left\lVert(\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}-1)X_{0}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}(1-\alpha_{1})d+(\sqrt{\alpha_{1}}-1)^{2}M_{2}d

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}(1-\alpha_{1})(M_{2}+1)d

where
(i)
follows from independence,
(ii)
follows from
1
, and
(iii)
follows because
(\sqrt{z}-1)^{2}\leq 1-z
for all
z\in[0,1]
. The proof is complete since
\mathrm{W}_{2}(Q_{0},Q_{1})^{2}\leq\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}\sim Q_{1},X_{0}\sim Q_{0}}\left\lVert X_{1}-X_{0}\right\rVert^{2}
by the definition of Wasserstein-2 distance.

Appendix G
Proof of
Theorems

5
,
2
,
3
and
4

In this section, we instantiate
Theorem

1
(along with
Corollary

1
) to provide upper bounds that have explicit parameter dependency for a number of interesting distribution classes.
In order to obtain an upper bound that explicitly depends on system parameters, we need only to provide an explicit bound on the reverse-step error, which is the main topic that we address in the following subsections.

G.1
Proof of
Theorem

2

We first introduce some relevant notations. Given that
Q_{0}
is Gaussian mixture, the p.d.f. of
q_{t}
at each time
t\geq 1
can be calculated as

\displaystyle q_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{0,n}(x_{0})\mathrm{d}x_{0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})q_{0,n}(x_{0})\mathrm{d}x_{0}=:\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x).

Since the convolution of two Gaussian density is still Gaussian, we have that
q_{t,n}
is the p.d.f. of
\mathcal{N}(\mu_{t,n},\Sigma_{t,n})
, where
\mu_{t,n}:=\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\mu_{0,n}
and
\Sigma_{t,n}:=\bar{\alpha}_{t}\Sigma_{0,n}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})I_{d}
. Note that
\Sigma_{t,n}
has full rank.

G.1.1
Checking
4

We first verify
4
for Gaussian mixture
Q_{0}
for any
\alpha_{t}
that satisfies
Definition

1
. The intuition is that its Gaussian-like tail (for all
t\geq 0
) is sufficient to control all higher-order derivatives of
\log q_{t}
.

In the following,
Lemma

13
provides an upper bound on any order of partial derivative of a Gaussian mixture density for any fixed
x_{t}
, as long as each mixture component is well controlled. This directly implies that the partial derivatives are also well controlled in expectation, and thus we verify
4
for Gaussian mixture in
Lemma

14
.

Lemma 13
.

Let
g(x|z)
be the conditional Gaussian p.d.f. of
\mathcal{N}(\mu_{z},\Sigma_{z})
. Define
q(x):=\int g(x|z)\mathrm{d}\Pi(z)
, where
\Pi(z)
is a mixing distribution (and denote
\mathcal{Z}
its support).
Suppose
b:=\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\left\lVert\mu_{z}\right\rVert<\infty
, and
suppose the following conditions on
\Sigma_{z}
hold for all
z\in\mathcal{Z}
:

1.

There exist
u,U\in\mathbb{R}
such that
u\leq\det(\Sigma_{z})\leq U
;

2.

There exists
V\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\left\lVert\Sigma_{z}^{-1}\right\rVert\leq V
;

3.

There exists
w\in\mathbb{R}
such that
\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z},i,j\in[d]^{2}}\left|[\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}]^{ij}\right|\leq w
.

Then,

\left|\partial_{\bm{a}}^{k}\log q(x)\right|\leq\min\left\{C^{k}B_{k}\frac{d^{2k}\max\left\{w,1\right\}^{k}}{u^{k/2}}U^{k}e^{k\frac{V}{2}(\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}+b^{2})},B_{k}\frac{d^{2k}\max\left\{w,1\right\}^{k}}{u^{k/2}}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{k}(x)\right|\right\},

where
B_{k}
is the Bell number,
C
is some constant, and
\mathrm{poly}_{k}(x)
is some
k
-th order polynomial in
x
.

Proof.

See
Section

H.1
.
∎

Lemma 14
.

When
Q_{0}
is Gaussian mixture (see
Theorem

2
),
4
is satisfied.

Proof.

See
Section

H.2
.
∎

G.1.2
Expressing
\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}

Now we continue from
Theorem

1
to work for an explicit dependency on
d
.
We first calculate the second partial derivative of its log-p.d.f. as

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{q_{t}^{2}(x)}\Bigg(q_{t}(x)\left(\sum_{n}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)\left(\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}(x-\mu_{t,n})(x-\mu_{t,n})^{\intercal}\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}-\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad-\left(\sum_{n}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}(x-\mu_{t,n})\right)\left(\sum_{n}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}(x-\mu_{t,n})\right)^{\intercal}\Bigg).

(40)

Now write
z_{t,n}(x):=\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}(x-\mu_{t,n})
. Note that
\partial_{k}z_{t,n}^{i}=[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ik}
, and that
\partial_{k}q_{t,n}(x)=q_{t,n}(x)(-z_{t,n}^{k}(x))
. We can rewrite (
G.1.2
) as

\displaystyle\partial^{2}_{ij}\log q_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{q_{t}^{2}(x)}\Bigg(q_{t}(x)\underbrace{\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)\left(z_{t,n}^{i}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)-[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ij}\right)}_{\text{N1}}

\displaystyle\qquad-\underbrace{\left(\sum_{n}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{i}(x)\right)\left(\sum_{n}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)\right)}_{\text{N2}}\Bigg).

To calculate the third partial derivative of its log-p.d.f., we need first to calculate the partial derivative of N1 and N2. The derivative for N1 is given by

\displaystyle\partial_{k}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)\left(z_{t,n}^{i}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)-[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ij}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)(-z_{t,n}^{k}(x))\left(z_{t,n}^{i}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)-[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ij}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ik}z_{t,n}^{j}(x)+\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{jk}z_{t,n}^{i}(x),

and the derivative for term N2 is given by

\displaystyle\partial_{k}\left(\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{i}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)\left((-z_{t,n}^{k}(x))z_{t,n}^{i}(x)+[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ik}\right)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{i}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)\left((-z_{t,n}^{k}(x))z_{t,n}^{j}(x)+[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{jk}\right).
