Combining these, the derivative for the numerator is

\displaystyle\partial_{k}(q_{t}(x)\text{N1}-\text{N2})=\partial_{k}(q_{t}(x))\text{N1}+q_{t}(x)\partial_{k}(\text{N1})-\partial_{k}(\text{N2})

\displaystyle=-\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{k}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)\left(z_{t,n}^{i}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)-[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ij}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+q_{t}(x)\Bigg(\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)(-z_{t,n}^{k}(x))\left(z_{t,n}^{i}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)-[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ij}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ik}z_{t,n}^{j}(x)+\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{jk}z_{t,n}^{i}(x)\Bigg)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)\left((-z_{t,n}^{k}(x))z_{t,n}^{i}(x)+[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ik}\right)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad-\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{i}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)\left((-z_{t,n}^{k}(x))z_{t,n}^{j}(x)+[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{jk}\right)

\displaystyle=-q_{t}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{i}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)z_{t,n}^{k}(x)-\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{k}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{i}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{i}(x)z_{t,n}^{k}(x)+\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{i}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)z_{t,n}^{k}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad+q_{t}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ij}z_{t,n}^{k}(x)+\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ij}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{k}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad+q_{t}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ik}z_{t,n}^{j}(x)-\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ik}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad+q_{t}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{jk}z_{t,n}^{i}(x)-\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{i}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{jk}.

Since

\displaystyle\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=\partial_{k}\left(\frac{q_{t}(x)\text{N1}-\text{N2}}{q_{t}^{2}(x)}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{q_{t}^{3}(x)}\left(\partial_{k}(q_{t}(x)\text{N1}-\text{N2})q_{t}(x)+2(q_{t}(x)\text{N1}-\text{N2})\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{k}(x)\right),

we get

\displaystyle q_{t}^{3}(x)\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(x)

\displaystyle=-q^{2}_{t}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{i}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)z_{t,n}^{k}(x)+q_{t}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{k}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{i}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad+q_{t}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{i}(x)z_{t,n}^{k}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad+q_{t}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{i}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)z_{t,n}^{k}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad-2\left(\sum_{n}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{i}(x)\right)\left(\sum_{n}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)\right)\left(\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{k}(x)\right)

\displaystyle\qquad+q_{t}^{2}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ij}z_{t,n}^{k}(x)-q_{t}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ij}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{k}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad+q_{t}^{2}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ik}z_{t,n}^{j}(x)-q_{t}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ik}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{j}(x)

\displaystyle\qquad+q_{t}^{2}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{jk}z_{t,n}^{i}(x)-q_{t}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)z_{t,n}^{i}(x)\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{jk}.

Below, we write
\xi_{t}(x,i):=\max_{n}\left|z_{t,n}^{i}(x)\right|
and
\bar{\Sigma}
to be a matrix such that
\bar{\Sigma}^{ij}:=\max_{n}\left|[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ij}\right|
. Also write
h_{t,n}(x)=\pi_{n}q_{t,n}(x)/q_{t}(x)
. Note that for any
x
,
\sum_{n=1}^{N}h_{t,n}(x)=1
.
Therefore, we take
\max_{n}
within each summation above and get

\left|\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(x)\right|\leq 6\xi_{t}(x,i)\xi_{t}(x,j)\xi_{t}(x,k)+2\bar{\Sigma}^{ij}\xi_{t}(x,k)+2\bar{\Sigma}^{ik}\xi_{t}(x,j)+2\bar{\Sigma}^{jk}\xi_{t}(x,i).

G.1.3
Asymptotic Equivalence of
\mu_{t}(x_{t})
and
x_{t}

Intuitively,
\mu_{t}(x_{t})
and
x_{t}
are asymptotically close when
1-\alpha_{t}
is small, which will be useful for later analysis. In this subsubsection, we will show that
\xi_{t-1}(\mu_{t},i)-\xi_{t}(x_{t},i)=\tilde{O}(1-\alpha_{t})
.

Note that for each
n
and fixed
x_{t}
(writing
\mu_{t}(x_{t})=\mu_{t}
),

\displaystyle z_{t-1,n}(\mu_{t})-z_{t,n}(x_{t})

\displaystyle=\Sigma_{t-1,n}^{-1}(\mu_{t}-\mu_{t-1,n})-\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}(x_{t}-\mu_{t,n})

\displaystyle=(\Sigma_{t-1,n}^{-1}-\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1})(\mu_{t}-\mu_{t-1,n})-\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}((x_{t}-\mu_{t,n})-(\mu_{t}-\mu_{t-1,n})).

(41)

Here, since
\Sigma_{t-1,n}
is real symmetric, we can write the eigen-decomposition as
\Sigma_{t-1,n}=UDU^{\intercal}
, where
U
is an orthonormal matrix (having unit 2-norm) and
D
is a diagonal matrix (with all diagonal elements positive). In the same notation,
\Sigma_{t-1,n}^{-1}=UD^{-1}U^{\intercal}
, and
\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}=(\alpha_{t}\Sigma_{t-1,n}+(1-\alpha_{t})I_{d})^{-1}=U(\alpha_{t}D+(1-\alpha_{t})I_{d})^{-1}U^{\intercal}
. Since

\displaystyle\left|[D^{-1}]^{ii}-[(\alpha_{t}D+(1-\alpha_{t})I_{d})^{-1}]^{ii}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\frac{1}{D^{ii}}-\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}D^{ii}+(1-\alpha_{t})}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})(\left|D^{ii}\right|+1)}{\alpha_{t}(D^{ii})^{2}+(1-\alpha_{t})D^{ii}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{O}(1-\alpha_{t}),

the following holds:

\left\lVert\Sigma_{t-1,n}^{-1}-\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}\right\rVert=\tilde{O}(1-\alpha_{t}).

Denote
[A]^{i*}
as the
i
-th row of a matrix
A
. Thus, following from (
G.1.3
), for any
i\in[d]
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert[\Sigma_{t-1,n}^{-1}]^{i*}-[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{i*}\right\rVert\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\left\lVert\Sigma_{t-1,n}^{-1}-\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\tilde{O}(1-\alpha_{t}),

(42)

\displaystyle\left|\mu_{t}^{i}-x_{t}^{i}\right|=\left|\frac{1-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}x_{t}^{i}-\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\partial_{i}\log q_{t}(x_{t})\right|

\displaystyle=\tilde{O}(1-\alpha_{t}),

\displaystyle\left|\mu_{t,n}^{i}-\mu_{t-1,n}^{i}\right|=\left|(1-\sqrt{\alpha_{t}})\mu_{t-1,n}^{i}\right|

\displaystyle=\tilde{O}(1-\alpha_{t}),

where
(i)
follows from the definition of matrix 2-norm and from the fact that
[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{i*}=\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}\bm{1}_{i}
(
\bm{1}_{i}
is the unit vector where the
i
-th element is 1, and recall that
\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}
is symmetric). This implies that
\left|z_{t-1,n}^{i}(\mu_{t})-z_{t,n}^{i}(x_{t})\right|=\tilde{O}(1-\alpha_{t}),\forall i
. Thus,

\displaystyle\xi_{t-1}(\mu_{t},i)-\xi_{t}(x_{t},i)

\displaystyle=\max_{n}\left|z_{t-1,n}^{i}(\mu_{t})\right|-\max_{n}\left|z_{t,n}^{i}(x_{t})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{n}\left|z_{t-1,n}^{i}(\mu_{t})-z_{t,n}^{i}(x_{t})\right|=\tilde{O}(1-\alpha_{t}),

(43)

where the last inequality follows because
\max_{n}|a_{n}|+\max_{n}|b_{n}|\geq\max_{n}(|a_{n}|+|b_{n}|)\geq\max_{n}|a_{n}+b_{n}|
.

Following from
Theorem

1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\Bigg[\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\Big(6\xi(\mu_{t}(X_{t}),i)\xi(\mu_{t}(X_{t}),j)\xi(\mu_{t}(X_{t}),k)+2\bar{\Sigma}^{ij}\xi(\mu_{t}(X_{t}),k)+2\bar{\Sigma}^{ik}\xi(\mu_{t}(X_{t}),j)

\displaystyle\qquad+2\bar{\Sigma}^{jk}\xi(\mu_{t}(X_{t}),i)\Big)\left(6\xi(X_{t},i)\xi(X_{t},j)\xi(X_{t},k)+2\bar{\Sigma}^{ij}\xi(X_{t},k)+2\bar{\Sigma}^{ik}\xi(X_{t},j)+2\bar{\Sigma}^{jk}\xi(X_{t},i)\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\asymp}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\left(\xi(X_{t},i)\xi(X_{t},j)\xi(X_{t},k)+\bar{\Sigma}^{ij}\xi(X_{t},k)+\bar{\Sigma}^{ik}\xi(X_{t},j)+\bar{\Sigma}^{jk}\xi(X_{t},i)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\xi(X_{t},i)^{2}\xi(X_{t},j)^{2}\xi(X_{t},k)^{2}+(\bar{\Sigma}^{ij})^{2}\xi(X_{t},k)^{2}+(\bar{\Sigma}^{ik})^{2}\xi(X_{t},j)^{2}+(\bar{\Sigma}^{jk})^{2}\xi(X_{t},i)^{2}\right]

(44)

where
(ii)
follows from (
G.1.3
).

G.1.4
Explicit Parameter Dependency

We are now ready for the explicit parameter dependency for Gaussian mixture
Q_{0}
. In the following, we provide two different ways to upper-bound the terms in (
G.1.3
) depending on how
N
is compared to
d
. The first approach can be applied when
N<d
. For the
\xi(x,\cdot)\penalty\ (\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d})
terms,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\xi(x,i)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\max_{n}([\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{i*}(x-\mu_{t,n}))^{2}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sum_{n=1}^{N}([\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{i*}(x-\mu_{t,n}))^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left\lVert\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}(x-\mu_{t,n})\right\rVert^{2}\leq N\max_{n}\left\lVert\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}\right\rVert^{2}\max_{n}\left\lVert x-\mu_{t,n}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\lesssim}}N\max_{n}\left\lVert x-\mu_{t,n}\right\rVert^{2},

where
(i)
follows because of the following.
Since
\Sigma_{t,n}
is a (full-rank) covariance matrix, all its eigenvalues are positive. Let
\lambda_{n,\text{min}}>0
be the smallest eigenvalue of
\Sigma_{0,n}
, and thus

\max_{n}\left\lVert\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}\right\rVert_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\bar{\alpha}_{t}\min_{n}\lambda_{n,\text{min}}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})}\leq\frac{1}{\min\{1,\min_{n}\lambda_{n,\text{min}}\}}<\infty.

(45)

In particular, this bound does not depend on
d
or
T
.
Also, for the
\bar{\Sigma}
terms,

\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}(\bar{\Sigma}^{ij})^{2}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\max_{n}([\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ij})^{2}\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}\sum_{n=1}^{N}([\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ij})^{2}=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\left\lVert\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}\right\rVert_{F}^{2}\lesssim Nd,

where the last inequality follows from (
45
) and the fact that for any matrix full-rank
A
,
\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{F}\leq\sqrt{d}\left\lVert A\right\rVert_{2}
.
The second approach can be applied when
N\geq d
, where we can bound the
\xi(x,\cdot)\penalty\ (\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d})
terms instead as

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\xi(x,i)^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\max_{n}([\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{i*}(x-\mu_{t,n}))^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\max_{n}\left(\left\lVert[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{i*}\right\rVert^{2}\left\lVert x-\mu_{t,n}\right\rVert^{2}\right)\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d}\max_{n}\left\lVert[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{i*}\right\rVert^{2}\max_{n}\left\lVert x-\mu_{t,n}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\max_{n}\left\lVert\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}\right\rVert^{2}\max_{n}\left\lVert x-\mu_{t,n}\right\rVert^{2}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iv)}}{{\lesssim}}d\max_{n}\left\lVert x-\mu_{t,n}\right\rVert^{2}.

Here
(ii)
follows from Cauchy-Schwartz inequality,
(iii)
follows from definition of matrix 2-norm and the fact that
[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{i*}=\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}\bm{1}_{i}
(
\bm{1}_{i}
is the unit vector where the
i
-th element is 1),
and
(iv)
follows from (
45
). Also, for the second term, we can obtain an alternative upper bound as follows.
Write the eigen-decomposition as
\Sigma_{0,n}=Q_{n}\mathrm{diag}(\lambda_{n,1},\dots,\lambda_{n,d})Q_{n}^{\intercal}
, where
Q_{n}
here is an orthonormal matrix (that does not depend on
T
). Then,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}

\displaystyle=Q_{n}(\bar{\alpha}_{t}\mathrm{diag}(\lambda_{n,1},\dots,\lambda_{n,d})+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})I_{d})^{-1}Q_{n}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle=Q_{n}\mathrm{diag}((\bar{\alpha}_{t}\lambda_{n,1}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}))^{-1},\dots,(\bar{\alpha}_{t}\lambda_{n,d}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}))^{-1})Q_{n}^{\intercal},

and thus

\displaystyle\max_{n\in[N]}\left|[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-1}]^{ij}\right|

\displaystyle=\max_{n\in[N]}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{d}(\bar{\alpha}_{t}\lambda_{n,k}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}))^{-1}Q_{n}^{ik}Q_{n}^{kj}\right|

\displaystyle\leq(\min\{1,\min_{n}\lambda_{n,\text{min}}\})^{-1}\max_{n\in[N],i,j\in[d]}\left|(Q_{n}^{i*})^{\intercal}(Q_{n}^{j*})\right|

\displaystyle\leq(\min\{1,\min_{n}\lambda_{n,\text{min}}\})^{-1}\max_{n\in[N],i\in[d]}\left\lVert Q_{n}^{i*}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=(\min\{1,\min_{n}\lambda_{n,\text{min}}\})^{-1},

where the last line follows because
Q_{n}
is orthonormal for all
n\in[N]
. Note that this is a uniform bound that does not depend on
N
,
T
or
d
, which further implies that

\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}(\bar{\Sigma}^{ij})^{2}\lesssim d^{2}.

Combining the two cases, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{d}\xi(x,i)^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim\min\{d,N\}\max_{n}\left\lVert x-\mu_{t,n}\right\rVert^{2},

(46)

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j=1}^{d}(\bar{\Sigma}^{ij})^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim d\min\{d,N\}.

(47)

Therefore, using (
46
) and (
47
), we can continue from (
G.1.3
) and get

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right]\\
\lesssim\min\{d,N\}^{3}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\left\lVert X_{t}\right\rVert^{6}+\max_{n}\left\lVert\mu_{t,n}\right\rVert^{6}\right]+(d\min\{d,N\})(d\min\{d,N\}).

Now, note that

\max_{n}\left\lVert\mu_{t,n}\right\rVert^{6}\leq\max_{n}\left\lVert\mu_{0,n}\right\rVert^{6}\lesssim d^{3}

since
\mu_{0,n}<\infty
is a fixed vector. Also, the expected sixth power of the norm can be bounded as

\mathbb{E}\left\lVert X_{t}\right\rVert^{6}=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\lVert\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}+\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\bar{W}_{t}\right\rVert^{2}\right)^{3}\right]\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left\lVert X_{0}\right\rVert^{6}+\mathbb{E}\left\lVert\bar{W}_{t}\right\rVert^{6}\lesssim\mathbb{E}\left\lVert X_{0}\right\rVert^{6}+d^{3},

and, when
Q_{0}
is a Gaussian mixture,

\int\left\lVert x_{0}\right\rVert^{6}q_{0}(x_{0})\mathrm{d}x_{0}=\sum_{n=1}^{N}\pi_{n}\int\left\lVert x_{0}\right\rVert^{6}q_{0,n}(x_{0})\mathrm{d}x_{0}\asymp d^{3}.

Therefore, we finally obtain a bound on the reverse-step error with explicit system parameters:

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1,t}}\left[\log\frac{q_{t-1|t}(X_{t-1}|X_{t})}{p^{\prime}_{t-1|t}(X_{t-1}|X_{t})}\right]\lesssim\frac{d^{3}\min\{d,N\}^{3}\log^{3}T}{T^{2}}.

G.2
Proof of
Theorem

3

Throughout the proof of
Theorem

3
we adopt the noise schedule
\alpha_{t}
defined in (
10
). We first investigate some nice properties of the noise schedule in (
10
). Since
c\asymp\log(1/\delta)
, we have
1-\alpha_{t}\lesssim\log(1/\delta)\log T/T
.
Using a similar argument from
[
13
, Equation (39)]
,

\displaystyle\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\bar{\alpha}_{t}},\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}},\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}\lesssim\frac{\log(1/\delta)\log T}{T},\quad\forall 2\leq t\leq T,

(48)

\displaystyle\frac{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}-1=\frac{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}(1-\alpha_{t})}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}\leq\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}=\tilde{O}\left(\frac{\log T}{T}\right),\quad\forall 2\leq t\leq T.

We note that
[
13
]
does not highlight
\delta
dependency in their results. Also, note that if
T
is large,

\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{\frac{T}{\log T}}\asymp\delta e^{c}\geq 1.

Thus, with any fixed
r\in(0,1)
such that
t\geq rT\penalty\ (\geq\frac{T}{\log T})
, we have

1-\alpha_{t}=\frac{c\log T}{T}\min\left\{\delta\left(1+\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{t},1\right\}=\frac{c\log T}{T}.

As a result,

\bar{\alpha}_{T}\leq\prod_{t=\left\lfloor rT\right\rfloor}^{T}\alpha_{t}=\left(1-\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)^{\left\lceil(1-r)T\right\rceil}\asymp\exp\left(\left\lceil(1-r)T\right\rceil\left(-\frac{c\log T}{T}\right)\right)=\tilde{O}(T^{-(1-r)c}).

(49)

Given any
c>2
, we can always find some
r
such that
(1-r)c>2
. For example, this is satisfied when
r=(c-2)/4
if
c\in(2,4)
and
r=1/4
otherwise. This shows that the
\alpha_{t}
in (
10
) satisfies
\bar{\alpha}_{T}=o\left(T^{-2}\right)
if
c>2
. Therefore, the
\alpha_{t}
in (
10
) satisfies
Definition

1
.

Since the parameter dependency is clear in the bound for the initialization and estimation errors (
Lemmas

3
and
4
), it remains to provide a bound on the reverse-step error that depends explicitly on the system parameters, which is the main topic below.

G.2.1
Checking
5

Instead of
4
, we check the more general
5
below. In particular, we verify
5
with the
\alpha_{t}
in (
10
).
In the following,
Lemma

15
is used to establish the first half of
5
. Next, the following
Lemma

16
is used to establish the behavior of the expected moments under the perturbed posterior
Q_{0|t-1}(\cdot|\mu_{t}(X_{t}))
when
X_{t}\sim Q_{t}
. Both
Lemmas

15
and
17
will be useful for establishing the second half of
5
with the
\alpha_{t}
in (
10
).

Lemma 15
.

For all
t\geq 1
,
\ell\geq 1
, and
\bm{a}\in[d]^{p}
such that
\left|\bm{a}\right|=p\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left|\partial_{\bm{a}}^{p}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right|^{\ell}\lesssim\frac{d^{p\ell/2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{p\ell/2}}.

Proof.

See
Section

H.3
.
∎

Lemma 16
.

For all
t\geq 2
and
p\geq 1
, with the
\alpha_{t}
in (
10
),

\int_{x_{0},x_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}(x_{t})-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}\right\rVert^{p}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t}(x_{t}))\mathrm{d}Q_{t}(x_{t})\lesssim d^{p/2}(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{p/2}.

Proof.

See
Section

H.4
.
∎

Finally, the following
Lemma

17
verifies the second half of
5
with the
\alpha_{t}
defined in (
10
).

Lemma 17
.

For all
t\geq 2
,
\ell\geq 1
, and
\bm{a}\in[d]
such that
\left|\bm{a}\right|=p\geq 1
, with the
\alpha_{t}
in (
10
),

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left|\partial_{\bm{a}}^{p}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\right|^{\ell}\lesssim\frac{d^{p\ell/2}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{p\ell/2}}.

Combining this with
Lemma

15
,
5
holds.

Proof.

See
Section

H.5
.
∎

Now,
5
is satisfied since
\frac{1}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\leq\frac{1}{1-\alpha_{1}}=\delta^{-1}
for all
t\geq 1
if
\delta
is constant. Thus, if
\delta
is a constant,
4
is already satisfied (as is
5
). This is not necessary, however, when
\delta=1/\mathrm{poly}(T)
is vanishing with
T
. Fortunately, in this case, from (
48
), we still get
\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}=\tilde{O}(1-\alpha_{t})
. Thus,
5
is still satisfied.

G.2.2
Expressing
\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}

We begin by investigating
\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}\penalty\ (t\geq 2)
, for which we can derive the Hessian of
\log q_{t}(x)
as

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x)=\frac{\partial}{\partial x}\left(\frac{\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})}{\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{q_{t}(x)\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla^{2}q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})-\left(\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\right)\left(\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\nabla q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\right)^{\intercal}}{q_{t}^{2}(x)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{2}q_{t}^{2}(x)}\bigg(q_{t}(x)\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})\left((x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0})(x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0})^{\intercal}-(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})I_{d}\right)\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})(x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0})\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\right)\left(\int_{x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})(x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0})\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\right)^{\intercal}\bigg)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}I_{d}+\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{2}}\bigg(\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0|t}(\cdot|x)}\left[(x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0})(x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0})^{\intercal}\right]

\displaystyle\quad-\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0|t}(\cdot|x)}\left[x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\right]\right)\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0|t}(\cdot|x)}\left[x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}\right]\right)^{\intercal}\bigg).

(50)

For the third-order partial derivatives, we employ the notation

z:=\frac{x-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}.

Note that
\partial_{k}q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})=q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})(-z^{k})
. Then, we can write (
G.2.2
) as

\partial^{2}_{ij}\log q_{t}(x)=\frac{1}{q_{t}^{2}(x)}\bigg(q_{t}(x)\underbrace{\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{i}z^{j}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})}_{\text{N1}}\\
-\underbrace{\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{i}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{j}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})}_{\text{N2}}\bigg)-\frac{1}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}I_{d}.

Note that the last term is a constant. The derivative for term N1 is given by

\displaystyle\partial_{k}\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{i}z^{j}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})(-z^{k})z^{i}z^{j}+\mathds{1}(k=i)q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}z^{j}

\displaystyle\qquad+\mathds{1}(k=j)q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}z^{i}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0}),

and the derivative for term N2 is given by

\displaystyle\partial_{k}\left(\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{i}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{j}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\right)

\displaystyle=\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})\left((-z^{k})z^{i}+\mathds{1}(k=i)(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}\right)\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{j}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{i}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})\left((-z^{k})z^{j}+\mathds{1}(k=j)(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}\right)\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=\left(\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})(-z^{k})z^{i}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})+\mathds{1}(k=i)(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}q_{t}(x)\right)\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{j}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{i}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\left(\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})(-z^{k})z^{j}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})+\mathds{1}(k=j)(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{-1}q_{t}(x)\right).

Combining these, the derivative for the numerator is given by

\displaystyle\partial_{k}(q_{t}(x)\text{N1}-\text{N2})=\partial_{k}(q_{t}(x))\text{N1}+q_{t}(x)\partial_{k}(\text{N1})-\partial_{k}(\text{N2})

\displaystyle=-q_{t}(x)\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{i}z^{j}z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad-\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{i}z^{j}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{j}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{i}z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{i}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{j}z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0}).

Thus,

\displaystyle\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(x)=\partial_{k}\frac{q_{t}(x)\text{N1}-\text{N2}}{q_{t}^{2}(x)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{q_{t}^{3}(x)}\left(\partial_{k}(q_{t}(x)\text{N1}-\text{N2})q_{t}(x)+2(q_{t}(x)\text{N1}-\text{N2})\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{q_{t}^{3}(x)}\bigg(-q_{t}^{2}(x)\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{i}z^{j}z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad+q_{t}(x)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{1}=i,j,k\\
a_{2}<a_{3},\penalty\ a_{2},a_{3}\neq a_{1}\end{subarray}}\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{a_{1}}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{a_{2}}z^{a_{3}}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle\qquad-2\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{i}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{j}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\int q_{t|0}(x|x_{0})z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})\bigg)

\displaystyle=-\int z^{i}z^{j}z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)

\displaystyle\qquad+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}a_{1}=i,j,k\\
a_{2}<a_{3},\penalty\ a_{2},a_{3}\neq a_{1}\end{subarray}}\int z^{a_{1}}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)\int z^{a_{2}}z^{a_{3}}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)

\displaystyle\qquad-2\int z^{i}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)\int z^{j}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)\int z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)

(51)

G.2.3
Explicit Parameter Dependency

By Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\Big(\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\Big)^{2}\right]}\times\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\Big(\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\Big)^{2}\right]}.

(52)

We now analyze the two terms in (
G.2.3
) separately.

We begin with the second term in (
G.2.3
). Recall that
Z=\frac{X_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}
is standard Gaussian under
Q_{0,t}
. Also note that for a standard Gaussian random variable
Z
,
\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert^{6}=d(d+2)(d+4)\lesssim d^{3}
.
Now, substituting (
G.2.2
) into the second term of (
G.2.3
), we get

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left(\int z^{i}z^{j}z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|X_{t})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{3}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0},X_{t}\sim Q_{0,t}}\left[\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\left(\frac{X_{t}^{i}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}^{i}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{X_{t}^{j}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}^{j}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{X_{t}^{k}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}^{k}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{3}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0},X_{t}\sim Q_{0,t}}\left\lVert\frac{X_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}\right\rVert^{6}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{3}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert^{6}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{d^{3}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{3}},

and

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left(\int z^{i}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)\int z^{j}z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\left\lVert\int z\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)\right\rVert^{2}\sum_{j,k=1}^{d}\left(\int z^{j}z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left\lVert\int z\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)\right\rVert^{6}\right)^{1/3}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left(\sum_{j,k=1}^{d}\left(\int z^{j}z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|x)\right)^{2}\right)^{3/2}\right)^{2/3}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{X_{0},X_{t}\sim Q_{0,t}}\left\lVert\frac{X_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\right\rVert^{6}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{3}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert^{6}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{d^{3}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{3}},

and

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left(\int z^{i}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|X_{t})\int z^{j}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|X_{t})\int z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|X_{t})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{d}\left(\int\frac{X_{t}^{i}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}^{i}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|X_{t})\right)^{2}\right)^{3}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{3}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left\lVert\int\frac{X_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|X_{t})\right\rVert^{6}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{3}}\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert^{6}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{d^{3}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{3}}.

Thus, the second term of (
G.2.3
) satisfies that

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\Big(\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\Big)^{2}\right]\lesssim\frac{d^{3}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{3}}.

Now we turn to the first term in (
G.2.3
). Note that
Z=\frac{\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}X_{0}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}
. While
Z
is no longer standard Gaussian under
Q_{0,t}
, we can still achieve moment bounds using
Lemma

16
.
Now, substituting (
G.2.2
) into the first term of (
G.2.3
), we apply
Lemma

16
and get

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left(\int z^{i}z^{j}z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{3}}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{0}\sim Q_{0|t-1}(\cdot|\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\\
X_{t}\sim Q_{t}\end{subarray}}\left\lVert\frac{\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}X_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\right\rVert^{6}\lesssim\frac{d^{3}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{3}},

and similarly,

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left(\int z^{i}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\int z^{j}z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\frac{d^{3}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{3}},

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left(\int z^{i}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\int z^{j}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\int z^{k}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim\frac{d^{3}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{3}}.

Thus, the first term of (
G.2.3
) satisfies that

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\Big(\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\Big)^{2}\right]\lesssim\frac{d^{3}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{3}}.

Finally, since
\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}},\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}\lesssim\frac{\log(1/\delta)\log T}{T}
, we arrive at

(1-\alpha_{t})^{3}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right]\lesssim\frac{d^{3}\log^{3}(1/\delta)\log^{3}T}{T^{3}}.

Summation over
t\geq 2
gives us the desirable result.

G.3
Theorem

5
and its Proof

Before we enter the proof of
Theorem

4
, we introduce an intermediate result which might have independent interest.
Previously, for regular samplers, linear dimensional dependency can be shown when all
Q_{t}
’s (
\forall t\geq 0
) have Lipschitz score
[
10
,
27
]
.
The following
Theorem

5
provides an accelerated convergence guarantee when
all

Q_{t}
’s (
\forall t\geq 0
) have Lipschitz Hessians.

Theorem 5

(Accelerated Sampler for All-Path Lipschitz Hessians)
.

Suppose that
\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x),\penalty\ \forall t\geq 0
is 2-norm
M
-Lipschitz, i.e.,
\exists M>0
such that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x)-\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(y)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq M\left\lVert x-y\right\rVert

(53)

for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
t\geq 0
.
Then, under
1
,
5
and
3
, if the
\alpha_{t}
satisfies
Definition

1
, the distribution
\widehat{P}^{\prime}_{0}
from the accelerated sampler satisfies

\mathrm{KL}(Q_{0}||\widehat{P}_{0}^{\prime})\lesssim\frac{d^{2}M^{2}\log^{3}T}{T^{2}}+(\log T)\varepsilon^{2}+\frac{\log^{2}T}{T}\varepsilon_{H}^{2}.

G.3.1
Proof of
Theorem

5

In order to continue from
Theorem

1
(in particular, the reverse-step error in (
26
)), we need to introduce some useful notations for the distribution class in (
53
). For a matrix
A
, define its vectorization as
\mathrm{vec}(A):=[A^{11},\dots,A^{1d},\dots,A^{d1},\dots,A^{dd}]^{\intercal}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}}
. Define
K_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d^{2}\times d}
to be the matrix that reorganizes the third-order partial derivative tensor, i.e.,

[K_{t}(x)]^{mk}:=\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(x),\penalty\ \text{s.t.}\penalty\ m=(i-1)d+j,\penalty\ \forall i,j,k\in[d].

With these notations, consider
y=x+\xi u
where
u\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
satisfies
\left\lVert u\right\rVert^{2}=1
and
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
is some small constant. Then,

\mathrm{vec}(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(y))-\mathrm{vec}(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x))=K_{t}(x^{*})(y-x)=\xi K_{t}(x^{*})u.

Here
x^{*}=\gamma x+(1-\gamma)y
for some
\gamma\in(0,1)
. Also, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathrm{vec}(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(y))-\mathrm{vec}(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x))\right\rVert

\displaystyle=\left\lVert\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(y)-\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x)\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{d}\left\lVert\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(y)-\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x)\right\rVert\leq\sqrt{d}M\left\lVert y-x\right\rVert

where the last inequality comes from (
53
). Thus, noting that
y=x+\xi u
and that
\left\lVert u\right\rVert^{2}=1
, we take the limit of
\xi
to 0 and get

\left\lVert K_{t}(x)\right\rVert\leq\sqrt{d}M,\quad\forall x\in\mathbb{R}^{d},\penalty\ \forall t\geq 0.

(54)

We now derive an explicit upper bound on the reverse-step error. Using Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, for any
t\geq 1
and
x_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(x_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}(\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}))^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}(\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(x_{t}))^{2}}

\displaystyle=\left\lVert K_{t-1}(\mu_{t})\right\rVert_{F}\times\left\lVert K_{t}(x_{t})\right\rVert_{F}

\displaystyle\leq(\sqrt{d}\left\lVert K_{t-1}(\mu_{t})\right\rVert)\times(\sqrt{d}\left\lVert K_{t}(x_{t})\right\rVert)

\displaystyle\leq d^{2}M^{2}.

(55)

Therefore, following from
Theorem

1
, we obtain

\sum_{t=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t-1},X_{t}\sim Q_{t-1,t}}\left[\log\frac{q_{t-1|t}(X_{t-1}|X_{t})}{p_{t-1|t}(X_{t-1}|X_{t})}\right]\lesssim\frac{d^{2}M^{2}\log^{3}T}{T^{2}}.

G.4
Proof of
Theorem

4

Throughout the proof of
Theorem

4
we adopt the noise schedule
\alpha_{t}
defined in (
10
) with
\delta=1/(M^{\frac{2}{3}}T^{\frac{3}{2}})
and
c\geq\log(M^{\frac{2}{3}}T^{\frac{3}{2}})
. Note that such
\alpha_{t}
satisfies
Definition

1
for all
t\geq 1
, and thus the bound on the estimation error still applies. Also,
5
is satisfied for
t\geq 2
, as shown in
Section

G.2.1
. Thus,
Theorem

3
can be applied and the reverse-step error at
t\geq 2
satisfies,
\forall t=T,\dots,2
,

(1-\alpha_{t})^{3}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right]\\
\lesssim\frac{d^{3}(\log^{3}M+\log^{3}T)\log^{3}T}{T^{3}}.

(56)

In order to determine the dimensional dependency of the reverse-step error, the key is thus to establish a similar upper bound at
t=1
.

Now, we provide a modified version of
Theorem

1
which does not require
q_{0}
to be analytic (as in
2
) or to have regular partial derivatives (as in
5
). We recall from (
D.3
) that the reverse-step error at time
t=1
can be upper-bounded as

\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0|1}}\left[\log\frac{q_{0|1}(X_{0}|x_{1})}{p^{\prime}_{0|1}(X_{0}|x_{1})}\right]\leq\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0|1}}[\zeta^{\prime}_{1,0}]-\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim P^{\prime}_{0|1}}[\zeta^{\prime}_{1,0}].

Instead of the Taylor expansion in (
D.3
), we employ the following different expansion from Taylor’s theorem. The only difference is that the expansion stops at the third-order term.

\displaystyle\zeta^{\prime}_{1,0}

\displaystyle=(\nabla\log q_{0}(\mu_{1})-\sqrt{\alpha_{0}}\nabla\log q_{1}(x_{1}))^{\intercal}(x_{0}-\mu_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{2}(x_{0}-\mu_{1})^{\intercal}\left(\nabla^{2}\log q_{0}(\mu_{1})-\frac{\alpha_{0}}{1-\alpha_{0}}B_{t}\right)(x_{0}-\mu_{1})

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{1}{3!}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{0}(\mu_{1}^{*})(x_{0}^{i}-\mu_{1}^{i})(x_{0}^{j}-\mu_{1}^{j})(x_{0}^{k}-\mu_{1}^{k}).

(57)

Here
\mu_{1}^{*}(x_{1},x_{0}):=\varsigma\mu_{1}(x_{1})+(1-\varsigma)x_{0}
for some
\varsigma\in[0,1]
. Note that
\mu_{1}^{*}
is a function of both
x_{1}
and
x_{0}
.

A remarkable difference from the proof of
Theorem

1
is that we do not require
q_{0}
to be analytic for this expansion. Indeed, it only requires that the third-order partial derivative exists.
With this new expansion, we have the following lemma, which serves as a counterpart of
Lemma

11
.

Lemma 18
.

Suppose that
q_{0}
exists and
\nabla^{2}\log q_{0}
is 2-norm
M
-Lipschitz. Then, with the
\alpha_{t}
in (
10
), we have

\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0|1}}-\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim P^{\prime}_{0|1}}\right)[\zeta^{\prime}_{1,0}]\lesssim\frac{(1-\alpha_{1})^{3/2}}{3!\alpha_{1}^{3/2}}d^{4}M.

Proof.

See
Section

H.6
.
∎

Finally, with the chosen
\delta=1-\alpha_{1}=1/(M^{\frac{2}{3}}T^{\frac{3}{2}})
, the rate at the first step satisfies

\frac{(1-\alpha_{1})^{3/2}}{3!\alpha_{1}^{3/2}}d^{4}M\lesssim\frac{d^{4}}{T^{9/4}}=o(T^{-2}).

As
T
becomes large, the rate of the total reverse-step error, which decays as
\tilde{O}(T^{-2})
, is not affected.
The proof is now complete.

Appendix H
Auxiliary Proofs of
Theorems

2
,
3
and
4

In this section, we provide the proofs for the lemmas in the proofs for
Theorems

2
,
3
and
4
.

H.1
Proof of
Lemma

13

Fix
k\geq 1
and
\bm{a}\in[d]^{k}
. Recall that
u\leq\det(\Sigma_{z})\leq U
,
\left\lVert\Sigma_{z}^{-1}\right\rVert\leq V
, and
\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z},i,j\in[d]^{2}}\left|[\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}]^{ij}\right|\leq w
for all
z\in\mathcal{Z}
.
Also write
\phi(y)
as the p.d.f. of the unit Gaussian.
We are interested in upper-bounding the absolute partial derivatives of
\log q(x)
with a function of
x
where

q(x)=\int g(x|z)\mathrm{d}\Pi(z),

where, using the change-of-variable formula,

g(x|z)=\frac{1}{\det(\Sigma_{z})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\phi\left(\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\mu_{z})\right).

(58)

We first identify an upper bound on the absolute partial derivatives of
q(x)
.
Now,

\displaystyle\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}q(x)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{=}}\int\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}g(x|z)\mathrm{d}\Pi(z)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{\inf_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\det(\Sigma_{z})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}\phi\left(\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\mu_{z})\right)\mathrm{d}\Pi(z)

where
(i)
follows from the dominated convergence theorem (see (
31
)), and
(ii)
follows from (
58
).
To obtain an upper bound on the
k
-th derivative of Gaussian density, we invoke the multivariate version of the Faá di Bruno’s formula
[
34
, Theorem 2.1]
. Since
y=\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\mu_{z})
is linear in
x
, only the first-order partial derivative is non-zero and is equal to an entry in
\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\left|\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}\phi\left(\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\mu_{z})\right)\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\sum_{\bm{a}^{\prime}\in[d]^{k}}\phi^{(k)}_{\bm{a}^{\prime}}(y)\prod_{s=1}^{k}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{a_{s}}}[\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\mu_{z})]^{a^{\prime}_{s}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\left|\sum_{\bm{a}^{\prime}\in[d]^{k}}\phi^{(k)}_{\bm{a}^{\prime}}\left(\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\mu_{z})\right)\right|\max\left\{w,1\right\}^{k},\penalty\ \forall\bm{a}:\left|\bm{a}\right|=k.

Here we define
\phi^{(k)}_{\bm{a}}(y):=\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}\phi(y)
. Since
\phi(y)
is a Gaussian density which is infinitely differentiable and decays exponentially at the tail, its
k
-th order derivative satisfies
\phi^{(k)}_{\bm{a}}(y)=\mathrm{poly}_{k}(y)\phi(y)
where
\mathrm{poly}_{k}(y)
is a
k
-th order polynomial function in
y_{1},\dots,y_{d}
(and thus in
x_{1},\dots,x_{d}
by linearity). Also note that, for any
\bm{a}\in[d]^{k}
,

\lim_{\left\lVert y\right\rVert\to\infty}\left|\phi^{(k)}_{\bm{a}}(y)\right|=\lim_{\left\lVert y\right\rVert\to\infty}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{k}(y)\phi(y)\right|=0.

By the continuity of
\phi^{(k)}_{\bm{a}}(y)
, there exists
\bar{y}_{\bm{a}}
such that
\left|\phi^{(k)}_{\bm{a}}(y)\right|\leq\left|\phi^{(k)}_{\bm{a}}(\bar{y}_{\bm{a}})\right|\leq\mathrm{poly}_{k}(\bar{y}_{\bm{a}})
for all
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Now, for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,

\displaystyle\left|\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}q(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\int\det(\Sigma_{z})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left|\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}\phi\left(\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\mu_{z})\right)\right|\mathrm{d}\Pi(z)

\displaystyle\leq\max\left\{w,1\right\}^{k}\int\det(\Sigma_{z})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\left(\sum_{\bm{a}\in[d]^{k}}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{k}\left(\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\mu_{z})\right)\right|\right)\phi\left(\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\mu_{z})\right)\mathrm{d}\Pi(z)

(59)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{d^{k}\max\left\{w,1\right\}^{k}}{\sqrt{u}}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{k}\left(\bar{y}_{\bm{a}}\right)\right|\phi\left(\bar{y}_{\bm{a}}\right).

(60)

We have thus obtained a constant upper bound on all partial derivatives of
q(x)
of order
k
.

Next, we convert the partial derivative bound into that for
\log q(x)
. We again invoke Faá di Bruno’s formula
[
34
]
. Note that

\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}\log q(x)=q(x)^{-k}\sum_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{k}}}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\partial^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}_{\bm{b_{j}}}q(x)=:\sum_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{k}}}r_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{k}}}(x)

(61)

in which we define each summation term as
r
.
Here
\{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{k}}\}
is some (possibly empty) partition of
\bm{a}
, i.e.,
\sum_{j}\bm{b_{j}}=\bm{a}
and
\sum_{j}\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|=k
(thus, at most
k
partitions). We order this partition such that
k\geq\left|\bm{b_{1}}\right|\geq\dots\geq\left|\bm{b_{k}}\right|\geq 0
.
Note that the total number of partition can be upper-bounded by
d^{k}\sum_{l=1}^{k}B_{k,l}(1,\dots,1)=d^{k}B_{k}
, where
B_{k,l}(\cdot)
and
B_{k}
are the Bell polynomials and the Bell number, respectively.

We first showcase a simple yet useful upper bound.
From (
60
), we get,

\displaystyle\left|\prod_{j=1}^{k}\partial^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}_{\bm{b_{j}}}q(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{j=1}^{k}\left|\partial^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}_{\bm{b_{j}}}q(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(d\max\{w,1\})^{\sum_{j}\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}}{\min\{u,1\}^{k/2}}\max\{\max_{y}\phi(y),1\}^{k}\prod_{j=1}^{k}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}(\bar{y}_{\bm{b_{j}}})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(d\max\{w,1\})^{\sum_{j}\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}}{\min\{u,1\}^{k/2}}\max\{\max_{y}\phi(y),1\}^{k}\max_{j}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}(\bar{y}_{\bm{b_{j}}})\right|^{k}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{j}}}^{k}\frac{d^{k}\max\left\{w,1\right\}^{k}}{\min\{u,1\}^{k/2}}

where, as noted above,
\bar{y}_{\bm{b_{j}}}
does not depend on
x
.
Here
C_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{j}}}
is some constant which depends only on the partition
\{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{j}}\}
and is independent of
x
.
On the other hand, we can also obtain a simple lower bound on
q(x)
. Observe that
q(x)
is continuous and always positive. Recall that
b=\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\left\lVert\mu_{z}\right\rVert
. Thus,

\displaystyle q(x)

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathcal{Z}}g(x|z)\mathrm{d}\Pi(z)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d/2}\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\det(\Sigma_{z})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{\mathcal{Z}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}(x-\mu_{z})^{\intercal}\Sigma_{z}^{-1}(x-\mu_{z})\right)\mathrm{d}\Pi(z)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d/2}\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\det(\Sigma_{z})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{\mathcal{Z}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\left\lVert\Sigma_{z}^{-1}\right\rVert(\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}+\left\lVert\mu_{z}\right\rVert^{2})\right)\mathrm{d}\Pi(z)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d/2}\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\det(\Sigma_{z})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\int_{\mathcal{Z}}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\sup_{z\in\mathcal{Z}}\left\lVert\Sigma_{z}^{-1}\right\rVert(\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}+b^{2})\right)\mathrm{d}\Pi(z)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{d/2}U}\exp\left(-\frac{V}{2}(\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}+b^{2})\right).

Therefore, if we set
C:=\max_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{j}}}C_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{j}}}
, we obtain

\left|\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}\log q(x)\right|\leq C^{k}B_{k}\frac{d^{2k}\max\left\{w,1\right\}^{k}}{\min\{u,1\}^{k/2}}U^{k}e^{k\frac{V}{2}(\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}+b^{2})}.

(62)

The upper bound above, though it depends only on parameters
u,U,V,w
, has an exponential dependency on
x
, which is not desirable. We next derive a more refined bound in
x
. For brevity of analysis, we re-express
r
(defined in (
61
)) to avoid empty partitions:

r_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{k}}}(x)=q(x)^{-p}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\partial^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}_{\bm{b_{j}}}q(x),\penalty\ \text{s.t.}\penalty\ \left|\bm{b_{p+1}}\right|=\dots=\left|\bm{b_{k}}\right|=0.

Now, by the boundedness of
\left\lVert\mu_{z}\right\rVert
and
\left\lVert\Sigma_{z}^{-1/2}\right\rVert
on
\mathcal{Z}
, for each
x
, there exist (bounded)
\bar{\Sigma}_{\bm{b_{j}}}
and
\bar{\mu}_{\bm{b_{j}}}
such that,
\forall z\in\mathcal{Z}
,

\sum_{\bm{b}\in[d]^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}\left(\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\mu_{z})\right)\right|\leq\sum_{\bm{b}\in[d]^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}\left(\bar{\Sigma}_{\bm{b_{j}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\bar{\mu}_{\bm{b_{j}}})\right)\right|<\infty.

Then, following from (
59
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|r_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{k}}}(x)\right|=q(x)^{-p}\left|\prod_{j=1}^{p}\partial^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}_{\bm{b_{j}}}q(x)\right|\leq q(x)^{-p}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left|\partial^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}_{\bm{b_{j}}}q(x)\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(d\max\{w,1\})^{\sum_{j=1}^{p}\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}}{u^{p/2}}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\prod_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\int\det(\Sigma_{z})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sum_{\bm{c_{j}}\in[d]^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}\left(\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\mu_{z})\right)\right|\phi\left(\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\mu_{z})\right)\mathrm{d}\Pi(z)}{\int\det(\Sigma_{z})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\phi\left(\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\mu_{z})\right)\mathrm{d}\Pi(z)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(d\max\{w,1\})^{k}}{\min\{1,u\}^{k/2}}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\prod_{j=1}^{p}\frac{\sum_{\bm{c_{j}}\in[d]^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}\left(\bar{\Sigma}_{\bm{b_{j}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\bar{\mu}_{\bm{b_{j}}})\right)\right|\left(\int\det(\Sigma_{z})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\phi\left(\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\mu_{z})\right)\mathrm{d}\Pi(z)\right)}{\int\det(\Sigma_{z})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\phi\left(\Sigma_{z}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\mu_{z})\right)\mathrm{d}\Pi(z)}

\displaystyle=\frac{(d\max\{w,1\})^{k}}{\min\{1,u\}^{k/2}}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\sum_{\bm{c_{j}}\in[d]^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}\left(\bar{\Sigma}_{\bm{b_{j}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\bar{\mu}_{\bm{b_{j}}})\right)\right|

Note that for each
j
, the number of terms in the summation above is upper-bounded by
d^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}
. Thus, expanding the product of summations would result in no more than
\prod_{j=1}^{p}d^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}=d^{k}
terms. Also, since
\left|\mathrm{poly}_{k_{1}}(y)\right|\cdot\left|\mathrm{poly}_{k_{2}}(y)\right|=\left|\mathrm{poly}_{k_{1}+k_{2}}(y)\right|
, and since any
\bar{\Sigma}_{\bm{b_{j}}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}(x-\bar{\mu}_{\bm{b_{j}}})
is linear in
x
and independent in
z
, each product term is a
k
-th order polynomial in
x
. Therefore, we obtain

\left|r_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{k}}}(x)\right|\leq\frac{d^{2k}\max\{w,1\}^{k}}{\min\{1,u\}^{k/2}}\max_{\bm{c_{j}}\in[d]^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|},\forall j=1,\dots,p}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{k}\left(x\right)\right|

and thus

\left|\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}\log q(x)\right|\leq B_{k}\frac{d^{2k}\max\{w,1\}^{k}}{\min\{1,u\}^{k/2}}\max_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{k}}}\max_{\bm{c_{j}}\in[d]^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|},\forall j=1,\dots,p}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{k}\left(x\right)\right|.

(63)

We have thus identified an upper bound on
\left|\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}\log q(x)\right|
which is polynomial in
x
.
The proof is now complete by combining (
62
) and (
63
).

H.2
Proof of
Lemma

14

We first identify
u,U,V,w
for
\Sigma_{t,n}
such that they are independent of
T
and
k
for all
t\geq 1
. Fix
t\geq 1
. We use the fact that
\Sigma_{t,n}=\bar{\alpha}_{t}\Sigma_{0,n}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})I_{d}
. If we let
\lambda_{n,1}\geq\dots\geq\lambda_{n,d}>0
as the eigenvalues of
\Sigma_{0,n}
(which do not depend on
T
), the eigenvalues of
\Sigma_{t,n}
are
\{\bar{\alpha}_{t}\lambda_{n,i}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})\}_{i=1}^{d}
. Therefore, for any
n=1,\dots,N
and
t\geq 1
,

(u:=)\prod_{i=1}^{d}\min\{\min_{n}\lambda_{n,i},1\}\leq\det(\Sigma_{t,n})\leq\prod_{i=1}^{d}\max\{\max_{n}\lambda_{n,i},1\}(=:U).

Also, following from (
45
), we have
V:=\frac{1}{\min\{1,\min_{n}\lambda_{n,d}\}}
.
Next, write the eigen-decomposition as
\Sigma_{0,n}=Q_{n}\mathrm{diag}(\lambda_{n,1},\dots,\lambda_{n,d})Q_{n}^{\intercal}
, where
Q_{n}
here is an orthonormal matrix (that does not depend on
T
). Then, for any
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\Sigma_{t,n}^{-\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=Q_{n}(\bar{\alpha}_{t}\mathrm{diag}(\lambda_{n,1},\dots,\lambda_{n,d})+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})I_{d})^{-\frac{1}{2}}Q_{n}^{\intercal}

\displaystyle=Q_{n}\mathrm{diag}((\bar{\alpha}_{t}\lambda_{n,1}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}))^{-\frac{1}{2}},\dots,(\bar{\alpha}_{t}\lambda_{n,d}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}))^{-\frac{1}{2}})Q_{n}^{\intercal}

and thus, for all
t\geq 1
,

\displaystyle[\Sigma_{t,n}^{-\frac{1}{2}}]^{ij}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{d}(\bar{\alpha}_{t}\lambda_{n,k}+(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}))^{-\frac{1}{2}}Q_{n}^{ik}Q_{n}^{kj}

\displaystyle\leq(\min\{1,\min_{n}\lambda_{n,d}\})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\max_{n\in[N],i,j\in[d]}\left|\sum_{k=1}^{d}Q_{n}^{ik}Q_{n}^{kj}\right|=:w.

Since the identified
u,U,V,w
are all independent of
T
and
k
, by
Lemma

13
we have obtained an upper bound on
\left|\partial^{k}_{\bm{a}}\log q(x)\right|
for any fixed
x
which is independent of
T
. Thus,

(1-\alpha_{t})^{k/2}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left|\partial_{\bm{a}}^{k}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right|,\penalty\ \penalty\ (1-\alpha_{t})^{k/2}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left|\partial_{\bm{a}}^{k}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\right|\\
=\tilde{O}\left((1-\alpha_{t})^{k/2}\right)=\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{T^{k/2}}\right).

Hence, we have shown
5
.

H.3
Proof of
Lemma

15

Fix
t\geq 1
. We will draw some notations introduced in
Lemma

13
. Specifically, we recall from (
61
) that

\displaystyle\partial^{p}_{\bm{a}}\log q_{t}(x_{t})

\displaystyle=q_{t}(x_{t})^{-p}\sum_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{p}}}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\partial^{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}_{\bm{b_{j}}}q_{t}(x_{t})

\displaystyle=q_{t}(x_{t})^{-p}\sum_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{p}}}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\int_{x_{0}}q_{t|0}(x_{t}|x_{0})\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}\left(\frac{x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\right)\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{p}}}\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{p}{2}}}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\int_{x_{0}}\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}\left(\frac{x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}\right)\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|x_{t})

(64)

in which we have defined
\mathrm{poly}_{k}(y)
as a
k
-th order polynomial function in
y_{1},\dots,y_{d}
.
Recall that here
\{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{p}}\}
is some (possibly empty) partition of
\bm{a}
, i.e.,
\sum_{j}\bm{b_{j}}=\bm{a}
and
\sum_{j}\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|=p
.

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left|\partial_{\bm{a}}^{p}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right|^{\ell}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{p\ell}{2}}}p^{\ell}\sum_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{p}}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left|\int_{x_{0}}\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}\left(\frac{X_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}\right)\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|X_{t})\right|^{\ell}\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{p\ell}{2}}}p^{\ell}\sum_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{p}}}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left|\int_{x_{0}}\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}\left(\frac{X_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}\right)\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t}(x_{0}|X_{t})\right|^{\frac{p\ell}{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}}\right)^{\frac{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}{p}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{p\ell}{2}}}p^{\ell}\sum_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{p}}}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{0},X_{t}\sim Q_{0,t}}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}\left(\frac{X_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}\right)\right|^{\frac{p\ell}{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}}\right)^{\frac{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}{p}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{p\ell}{2}}}p^{\ell}\sum_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{p}}}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left(\mathbb{E}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}\left(Z\right)\right|^{\frac{p\ell}{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}}\right)^{\frac{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}{p}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{d^{\frac{p\ell}{2}}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{\frac{p\ell}{2}}}

where
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
is a standard Gaussian random variable (that does not depend on
T
here) and any
r
-th order of polynomial of
Z_{1},\dots,Z_{d}
has finite expectation (that does not depend on
T
and with at most
d^{r/2}
dimensional dependency).
Here
(i)
holds by Hölder’s inequality, and
(ii)
holds by Jensen’s inequality since
p\ell/\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|\geq 1
for all
\bm{b_{j}}
and
\ell\geq 1
.
The proof is now complete.

H.4
Proof of
Lemma

16

Fix
t\geq 2
. We first introduce the following notations. Write
\mu_{t}=\mu_{t}(x_{t})
. Let
Q_{\mu_{t}}
be the distribution of
\mu_{t}(X_{t})
where
X_{t}\sim Q_{t}
, and let
q_{\mu_{t}}
be the corresponding p.d.f. (w.r.t. the Lebesgue measure). Let
Q_{\mu_{t},x_{0}}
be the joint distribution of
\mu_{t}
and
x_{0}
.

Now, we can re-write the integral as

\displaystyle\int_{x_{0},x_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}(x_{t})-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}\right\rVert^{p}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t}(x_{t}))\mathrm{d}Q_{t}(x_{t})

\displaystyle=\int_{x_{0},\mu_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}\right\rVert^{p}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t})\mathrm{d}Q_{\mu_{t}}(\mu_{t})

\displaystyle=\int_{x_{0},\mu_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}\right\rVert^{p}\frac{q_{\mu_{t}}(\mu_{t})}{q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t})\mathrm{d}Q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\int_{x_{0},\mu_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}\right\rVert^{2p}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t})\mathrm{d}Q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\sqrt{\int_{x_{0},\mu_{t}}\left(\frac{q_{\mu_{t}}(\mu_{t})}{q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})}\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t})\mathrm{d}Q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})}

(65)

where the last line follows from Cauchy-Schwartz inequality.

Now, for the first term of (
H.4
) we recovered the matched moment, and we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{\int_{x_{0},\mu_{t}}\left\lVert\mu_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}\right\rVert^{2p}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t})\mathrm{d}Q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\int_{x_{0},x_{t-1}}\left\lVert x_{t-1}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}\right\rVert^{2p}\mathrm{d}Q_{0,t-1}(x_{0},x_{t-1})}

\displaystyle=(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sqrt{\int_{x_{0},x_{t-1}}\left\lVert\frac{x_{t-1}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\right\rVert^{2p}\mathrm{d}Q_{0,t-1}(x_{0},x_{t-1})}

\displaystyle=(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{\frac{p}{2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left\lVert Z\right\rVert^{2p}}\lesssim d^{\frac{p}{2}}(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{\frac{p}{2}}

where
Z\sim\mathcal{N}(0,I_{d})
is a Gaussian random variable.

Now we upper bound the second term in (
H.4
), whose square is equal to

\displaystyle\int_{x_{0},\mu_{t}}\left(\frac{q_{\mu_{t}}(\mu_{t})}{q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})}\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t})\mathrm{d}Q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})

\displaystyle=\int_{x_{t-1}}\left(\frac{q_{\mu_{t}}(x_{t-1})}{q_{t-1}(x_{t-1})}\right)^{2}q_{t-1}(x_{t-1})\mathrm{d}x_{t-1}

\displaystyle=1+\chi^{2}(Q_{\mu_{t}}||Q_{t-1})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}1+\chi^{2}(Q_{\mu_{t},x_{0}}||Q_{t-1,0})

\displaystyle=\int_{x_{0}}\left(\int_{\mu_{t}}\left(\frac{q_{\mu_{t}|x_{0}}(\mu_{t}|x_{0})}{q_{t-1|0}(\mu_{t}|x_{0})}\right)^{2}q_{t-1|0}(\mu_{t}|x_{0})\mathrm{d}\mu_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle=\int_{x_{0}}\left(\int_{x_{t}}\frac{(q_{t|0}(x_{t}|x_{0}))^{2}}{q_{t-1|0}(\mu_{t}(x_{t})|x_{0})}\det\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{t}(x_{t})}{\mathrm{d}x_{t}}\right)^{-1}\mathrm{d}x_{t}\right)\mathrm{d}Q_{0}(x_{0})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{\leq}}\sqrt{\int_{x_{0},x_{t}}\left(\frac{q_{t|0}(x_{t}|x_{0})}{q_{t-1|0}(\mu_{t}(x_{t})|x_{0})}\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}Q_{t,0}(x_{t},x_{0})}\times

\displaystyle\qquad\sqrt{\int_{x_{0},x_{t}}\det\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{t}(x_{t})}{\mathrm{d}x_{t}}\right)^{-2}\mathrm{d}Q_{t,0}(x_{t},x_{0})}

where
\chi^{2}(P||Q)
is the chi-squared divergence between
P
and
Q
. Here
(i)
follows from the data processing inequality for f-divergence, and
(ii)
again follows from Cauchy-Schwartz inequality. We can calculate the determinant term above as

\displaystyle\det\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{t}}{\mathrm{d}x_{t}}\right)^{-2}

\displaystyle=\det\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}I_{d}+\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\sqrt{\alpha_{t}}}\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t})\right)^{-2}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}^{\frac{d}{2}}}\left(1+(1-\alpha_{t})\mathrm{Tr}(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t}))+\epsilon_{T}(x_{t})\right)\right)^{-2}

\displaystyle\leq\alpha_{t}^{\frac{d}{2}}\left(1-2(1-\alpha_{t})\mathrm{Tr}(\nabla^{2}\log q_{t}(x_{t}))+\epsilon_{T}(x_{t})\right)

where we denote the residual terms as
\epsilon_{T}(x_{t}):=\sum_{p=2}^{\infty}(1-\alpha_{t})^{p}\sum_{\bm{I}:|\bm{I}|=p}c_{\bm{I}}\prod_{(i,j)\in\bm{I}}\partial^{2}_{ij}\log q_{t}(x_{t})
, where
c_{\bm{I}}
is some coefficient that does not depend on
T
.
Since from
Lemma

15
,

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left|\partial^{2}_{ij}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right|^{\ell}=\tilde{O}\left(\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{\ell}}\right),\quad\forall i,j\in[d],\penalty\ \forall\ell\geq 1,

and note that
\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}=\tilde{O}\left(\frac{\log T}{T}\right)
with the
\alpha_{t}
in (
10
), we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left|\epsilon_{T}(X_{t})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p=2}^{\infty}(1-\alpha_{t})^{p}\sum_{\bm{I}:|\bm{I}|=p}c_{\bm{I}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\prod_{(i,j)\in\bm{I}}\left|\partial^{2}_{ij}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p=2}^{\infty}(1-\alpha_{t})^{p}\sum_{\bm{I}:|\bm{I}|=p}c_{\bm{I}}\prod_{(i,j)\in\bm{I}}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left|\partial^{2}_{ij}\log q_{t}(X_{t})\right|^{p}\right)^{\frac{1}{p}}

\displaystyle=\sum_{p=2}^{\infty}\tilde{O}\left(\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})^{p}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{p}}\right)

\displaystyle=\tilde{O}\left(\frac{(\log T)^{2}}{T^{2}}\right),

and thus

\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\det\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu_{t}}{\mathrm{d}x_{t}}\right)^{-2}=\alpha_{t}^{\frac{d}{2}}+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{\log T}{T}\right)\leq 1+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{\log T}{T}\right).

Also, since

\displaystyle\left(\frac{q_{t|0}(x_{t}|x_{0})}{q_{t-1|0}(\mu_{t}|x_{0})}\right)^{2}=\frac{\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t})^{d}}\exp\left(-\frac{\left\lVert x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\right\rVert^{2}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\right)}{\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{d}}\exp\left(-\frac{\left\lVert x_{t}+(1-\alpha_{t})\nabla\log q_{t}(x_{t})-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\right\rVert^{2}}{\alpha_{t}-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\right)^{d}\exp\left(\left\lVert x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\right\rVert^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\alpha_{t}-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}-\frac{1}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\right)\right)\times

\displaystyle\qquad\exp\left(\frac{2(1-\alpha_{t})\nabla\log q_{t}(x_{t})^{\intercal}(x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0})+(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\left\lVert\nabla\log q_{t}(x_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}{\alpha_{t}-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\leq}}\exp\left(\left\lVert\frac{x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}\right\rVert^{2}\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\right)\times

\displaystyle\qquad\exp\left(\frac{2(1-\alpha_{t})\nabla\log q_{t}(x_{t})^{\intercal}(x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0})+(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\left\lVert\nabla\log q_{t}(x_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}{\alpha_{t}-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iv)}}{{=}}\exp\left(\left\lVert\frac{x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}\right\rVert^{2}\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\right)\times

\displaystyle\qquad\left(1+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\nabla\log q_{t}(x_{t})^{\intercal}(x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0})+(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\left\lVert\nabla\log q_{t}(x_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}{\alpha_{t}-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\right)\right)

where
(iii)
follows because
\frac{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}<1
, and
(iv)
follows because
e^{z}=1+\tilde{O}(z)
when
z\to 0
and because
\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\bar{\alpha}_{t}},\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}=\tilde{O}\left(\frac{\log T}{T}\right)
with the
\alpha_{t}
in (
10
). Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X_{0}\sim Q_{t,0}}\left(\frac{q_{t|0}(X_{t}|X_{0})}{q_{t-1|0}(\mu_{t}(X_{t})|X_{0})}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X_{0}\sim Q_{t,0}}\exp\left(2\left\lVert\frac{X_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}\right\rVert^{2}\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\right)}\times

\displaystyle\sqrt{1+\tilde{O}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X_{0}\sim Q_{t,0}}\left[\frac{(1-\alpha_{t})\left\lVert\nabla\log q_{t}(x_{t})\right\rVert\left\lVert x_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}x_{0}\right\rVert+(1-\alpha_{t})^{2}\left\lVert\nabla\log q_{t}(x_{t})\right\rVert^{2}}{\alpha_{t}-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\right]\right)}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(v)}}{{=}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X_{0}\sim Q_{t,0}}\exp\left(2\left\lVert\frac{X_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}\right\rVert^{2}\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\right)}\times\left(1+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{\log T}{T}\right)\right)

where
(v)
follows from
Lemma

15
and Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, and

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t},X_{0}\sim Q_{t,0}}\exp\left(2\left\lVert\frac{X_{t}-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t}}X_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}}\right\rVert^{2}\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\int_{z}e^{2\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{\alpha_{t}-\bar{\alpha}_{t}}\left\lVert z\right\rVert^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert z\right\rVert^{2}}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{\frac{d}{2}}}\int_{z}e^{-\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert z\right\rVert^{2}(1+\tilde{O}(\log T/T))}\mathrm{d}z

\displaystyle=1+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{\log T}{T}\right).

Therefore, we arrive at a bound for the second term in (
H.4
):

\sqrt{\int_{x_{0},\mu_{t}}\left(\frac{q_{\mu_{t}}(\mu_{t})}{q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})}\right)^{2}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t})\mathrm{d}Q_{t-1}(\mu_{t})}\leq 1+\tilde{O}\left(\frac{\log T}{T}\right).

and the lemma follows immediately.

H.5
Proof of
Lemma

17

Fix
t\geq 2
. From (
H.3
), we also have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left|\partial_{\bm{a}}^{p}\log q_{t-1}(\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\right|^{\ell}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{\frac{p\ell}{2}}}p^{\ell}\sum_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{p}}}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left[\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left|\int_{x_{0}}\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}\left(\frac{\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\right)\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\right|^{\ell}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{\frac{p\ell}{2}}}p^{\ell}\sum_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{p}}}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\left|\int_{x_{0}}\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}\left(\frac{\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\right)\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\right|^{\frac{p\ell}{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}}\right)^{\frac{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{\frac{p\ell}{2}}}p^{\ell}\sum_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{p}}}\prod_{j=1}^{p}\left(\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\int_{x_{0}}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}\left(\frac{\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\right)\right|^{\frac{p\ell}{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t}(X_{t}))\right)^{\frac{\left|\bm{b_{j}}\right|}{p}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{\frac{p\ell}{2}}}p^{\ell}\sum_{\bm{b_{1}},\dots,\bm{b_{p}}}\max_{j\in[p]}\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\int_{x_{0}}\left|\mathrm{poly}_{p\ell}\left(\frac{\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\right)\right|\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t}(X_{t}))

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{\frac{p\ell}{2}}}\cdot\mathbb{E}_{X_{t}\sim Q_{t}}\int_{x_{0}}\left\lVert\frac{\mu_{t}(X_{t})-\sqrt{\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}x_{0}}{\sqrt{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}}\right\rVert^{p\ell}\mathrm{d}Q_{0|t-1}(x_{0}|\mu_{t}(X_{t}))

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{d^{\frac{p\ell}{2}}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1})^{\frac{p\ell}{2}}}

where the last line follows from
Lemma

16
.
Now, together with
Lemma

15
,
5
is established noting that
\frac{1-\alpha_{t}}{1-\bar{\alpha}_{t-1}}=\tilde{O}\left(\frac{\log T}{T}\right)=\tilde{O}(1-\alpha_{t})
for all
t\geq 2
.

H.6
Proof of
Lemma

18

Recall the expansion of
\zeta^{\prime}_{1,0}
in (
G.4
). As in the proof of
Lemma

11
, with the choice of
\mu_{1}
and
\Sigma_{1}
, we still have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim P^{\prime}_{0|1}}[T_{1}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0|1}}[T_{1}],

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim P^{\prime}_{0|1}}[T^{\prime}_{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{X_{0}\sim Q_{0|1}}[T^{\prime}_{2}].

Define
T_{3}^{\prime}:=\frac{1}{3!}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{0}(\mu_{1}^{*})(x_{0}^{i}-\mu_{1}^{i})(x_{0}^{j}-\mu_{1}^{j})(x_{0}^{k}-\mu_{1}^{k})
. Here
\mu_{1}^{*}=\mu_{1}^{*}(x_{1},x_{0})
is a function of both
x_{1}
and
x_{0}
. A useful result from
Lemma

15
is that, with the
\alpha_{t}
in (
10
), we have,
\forall i,j,k\in[d]
and
\ell\geq 1
,

\displaystyle(1-\alpha_{1})^{\ell}\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}\sim Q_{1}}\left|\partial^{2}_{ij}\log q_{1}(X_{1})\right|^{\ell}\lesssim\frac{(1-\alpha_{1})^{\ell}d^{\ell}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{1})^{\ell}}=d^{\ell},

(66)

\displaystyle(1-\alpha_{1})^{3}\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}\sim Q_{1}}\left|\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{1}(X_{1})\right|^{2}\lesssim\frac{(1-\alpha_{1})^{3}d^{3}}{(1-\bar{\alpha}_{1})^{3}}=d^{3}.

(67)

First, using
Lemma

8
, we have that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{X_{0},X_{1}\sim Q_{0,1}}\left[T_{3}^{\prime}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{(1-\alpha_{1})^{3}}{3!\alpha_{1}^{3/2}}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{X_{0},X_{1}\sim Q_{0,1}}[\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{0}(\mu_{1}^{*}(X_{1},X_{0}))\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{1}(X_{1})]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-\alpha_{1})^{3}}{3!\alpha_{1}^{3/2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{0},X_{1}\sim Q_{0,1}}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}(\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{0}(\mu_{1}^{*}(X_{1},X_{0})))^{2}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}\sim Q_{1}}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}(\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{1}(X_{1}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{(1-\alpha_{1})^{3}}{3!\alpha_{1}^{3/2}}dM\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}\sim Q_{1}}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}(\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{1}(X_{1}))^{2}}.

Here in the last line we have used a similar technique in (
G.3.1
), which assumes that
\nabla^{2}\log q_{0}
is 2-norm
M
-Lipschitz. Now, from (
67
) we have

\mathbb{E}_{X_{0},X_{1}\sim Q_{0,1}}\left[T_{3}^{\prime}\right]\lesssim\frac{(1-\alpha_{1})^{3/2}}{3!\alpha_{1}^{3/2}}d^{4}M.

Also,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{0}\sim P^{\prime}_{0|1}\\
X_{1}\sim Q_{1}\end{subarray}}\left[T_{3}^{\prime}\right]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{3!}\sum_{i,j,k=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{0}\sim P^{\prime}_{0|1}\\
X_{1}\sim Q_{1}\end{subarray}}\left[\partial^{3}_{ijk}\log q_{0}(\mu_{1}^{*}(X_{1},X_{0}))\prod_{c=i,j,k}(X_{0}^{c}-\mu_{1}^{c}(X_{1}))\right]

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(i)}}{{\leq}}\frac{1}{3!}dM\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{0}\sim P^{\prime}_{0|1}\\
X_{1}\sim Q_{1}\end{subarray}}\left\lVert X_{0}-\mu_{1}(X_{1})\right\rVert^{6}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{3!}d^{2}M\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{E}_{\begin{subarray}{c}X_{0}\sim P^{\prime}_{0|1}\\
X_{1}\sim Q_{1}\end{subarray}}\left(X_{0}^{i}-\mu_{1}(X_{1})^{i}\right)^{6}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(ii)}}{{=}}\frac{1}{3!}d^{2}M\sqrt{\sum_{i=1}^{d}15\left(\frac{1-\alpha_{1}}{\alpha_{1}}\right)^{3}\mathbb{E}_{X_{1}\sim Q_{1}}\left(1+(1-\alpha_{1})\partial^{2}_{ii}\log q_{1}(X_{1})\right)^{3}}

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(iii)}}{{\lesssim}}\frac{(1-\alpha_{1})^{3/2}}{3!\alpha_{1}^{3/2}}d^{4}M

where
(i)
holds with a similar technique in (
G.3.1
) assuming
\nabla^{2}\log q_{0}
is
M
-Lipschitz,
(ii)
holds by
Lemma

7
, and
(iii)
holds by (
66
).
The proof is now complete.
